containing many cone-points as a concatenation of “irreducible graphs” by splitting it at its regular cone-points. This will lead to a structure that, graphically, looks like a renewal structure. This is what we will do in this subsection. The last step will then be to extract a
real
renewal structure (at the level of the measure) from this graphical one. This will be Subsection
7.7
. To this end, introduce the notion of
irreducible graphs
. Let
\mathrm{e}=\{\pm\mathrm{e}_{1}\}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}
if the intersection is not empty, and
\mathrm{e}=\{\pm\mathrm{e}_{2}\}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}
else. Say that

•

A marked backward-confined graph
(\gamma_{L},v^{*})
is
irreducible
if the diamond
\mathrm{Diamond}(v^{*},\mathbf{b}(\gamma_{L}))
does not contain a regular cone-point of
\gamma_{L}
, and
\mathbf{b}(\gamma_{L})
is a regular cone-point of the graph
\gamma_{L}\cup\{\mathbf{b}(\gamma_{L}),\mathbf{b}(\gamma_{L})+\mathrm{e}\}
(see Fig.
19
).

•

A marked forward-confined graph
(\gamma_{R},v^{*})
is
irreducible
if the diamond
\mathrm{Diamond}(\mathbf{f}(\gamma_{R}),v^{*})
does not contain a regular cone-point of
\gamma_{R}
, and
\mathbf{f}(\gamma_{R})
is a regular cone-point of the graph
\gamma_{R}\cup\{\mathbf{f}(\gamma_{R}),\mathbf{f}(\gamma_{R})-\mathrm{e}\}
.

•

A diamond-confined graph
\gamma
is
irreducible
if it does not contain a regular cone-point, and if
\mathbf{f}(\gamma),\mathbf{b}(\gamma)
are regular cone-points of
\gamma\cup\{\{\mathbf{f}(\gamma),\mathbf{f}(\gamma)-\mathrm{e}\},\{\mathbf{b}(\gamma),\mathbf{b}(\gamma)+\mathrm{e}\}\}
(see Fig.
19
).

In words: irreducible graphs are those that cannot be written as the concatenation of two non-trivial graphs, with the concatenation point being a regular cone-point. We will denote the sets of irreducible marked graphs in, respectively,
\mathfrak{B}_{L}
,
\mathfrak{B}_{R}
,
\mathfrak{A}
by

\mathfrak{B}_{L}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}},\quad\mathfrak{B}_{R}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}},\quad\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}.

Figure 19.
From left to right. 1) Backward confined graph which is not irreducible: the graph can be written as the concatenation of two graphs so that the concatenation point is a regular cone-point. 2) The graph is not irreducible as its endpoints will not generate regular cone-points when concatenated. 3) The graph is not irreducible as it contains a regular cone-point. 4) An irreducible graph.

For
x\in\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, and
C\ni x
a realization of
\mathcal{C}
containing at least two regular cone points
v_{1},v_{2}
with
0\in v_{i}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright}
,
x\in v_{i}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}
, we can introduce the splitting into irreducible components:

C=\eta_{L}\sqcup\eta_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\eta_{M}\sqcup\eta_{R},

where
M\geq 1
,
(\eta_{L},0)
,
(\eta_{R},x)
,
\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{M}
are all irreducible, confined, (marked) graphs, and
\sqcup
means disjoint union of edges; note that there are sites overlap at the cone-points. Now, as mentioned in the end of Section
7.1
, there is a bijection between pairs
(\tilde{\gamma},v)\in\mathfrak{A}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and diamond-confined connected graphs: translate
\tilde{\gamma}
by
v
to obtain the graph
\gamma=v+\tilde{\gamma}
. Similar considerations hold for marked forward/backward confined connected graphs. In particular, for
\eta_{i}
in the above decomposition, there is a unique
w_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and a unique
\tilde{\eta}_{i}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
such that
\eta_{i}=w_{i}+\tilde{\eta}_{i}
. Similarly for
\eta_{L},\eta_{R}
. As the marked point of
\eta_{L}
is
0
, one has directly

w_{L}=0,\quad w_{1}=\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{L}),\quad w_{2}=\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{L}\circ\tilde{\eta}_{1}),\dots\quad w_{R}=\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{L}\circ\tilde{\eta}_{1}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{M}),

and the equivalent writing of
C
:

\eta_{L}\sqcup\eta_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\eta_{M}\sqcup\eta_{R}=\tilde{\eta}_{L}\circ\tilde{\eta}_{1}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{M}\circ\tilde{\eta}_{R},\qquad\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{L}\circ\tilde{\eta}_{1}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{R})=x,

with
\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{M}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
,
\tilde{\eta}_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
, and
\tilde{\eta}_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
.

By our definition of “regular cone points”, each
\tilde{\eta}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
contains a unique edge incident to
\mathbf{f}(\tilde{\eta})
, and the same for
\mathbf{b}(\tilde{\eta})
. Denote these edges by
\mathbf{f}_{e}(\tilde{\eta}),\mathbf{b}_{e}(\tilde{\eta})
.
For
\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\eta}_{L},\tilde{\eta}_{R}
as above, and
v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, introduce the percolation events
A_{v}(\tilde{\eta})
stating that the following occurs:

•

the edges in
(v+\tilde{\eta}\setminus\{\mathbf{f}_{e}(\tilde{\eta}),\mathbf{b}_{e}(\tilde{\eta})\})
are open in
\omega_{\tau}
,

•

v+\mathbf{f}_{e}(\tilde{\eta})
, and
v+\mathbf{b}_{e}(\tilde{\eta})
are open in
\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}
and closed in
\omega_{\tau}
, and

•

\partial^{\mathrm{ex}}(v+\tilde{\eta})
are closed in
\omega_{\tau}
.

Define the edges
\mathbf{b}_{e}(\tilde{\eta}_{L})
,
\mathbf{f}_{e}(\tilde{\eta}_{R})
, and the events
A_{v}(\tilde{\eta}_{L})
,
A_{v}(\tilde{\eta}_{R})
similarly. See Fig.
20
.

Figure 20.
Top: A cluster with its irreducible decomposition. Bottom: the corresponding percolation events. Bold black edges are open in
\omega_{\tau}
(hence also in
\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}
); golden edges are closed in
\omega_{\tau}
but open in
\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}
; thin lines are the dual of edges that are closed in
\omega_{\tau}
, but for which the state of
\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}
is not prescribed.

Claim 7.11
.

Define
c_{J,U}:=\big(\frac{\sinh(2J)}{e^{-2J}-e^{-2U}}\big)^{2}
. Then, one has

\Phi(\mathcal{C}=\eta_{L}\sqcup\eta_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\eta_{M}\sqcup\eta_{R})=c_{J,U}^{(M+1)}\Phi\big(A_{w_{L}}(\tilde{\eta}_{L}),A_{w_{1}}(\tilde{\eta}_{1}),\dots,A_{w_{R}}(\tilde{\eta}_{R})\big).

Proof.

Under
\mathcal{C}=\eta_{L}\sqcup\eta_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\eta_{M}\sqcup\eta_{R}
, closing any edge of the form
\mathbf{b}_{e},\mathbf{f}_{e}
cuts the cluster into two parts.
Thus, changing the state of such an edge in
\omega_{\tau},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}
from
(1,1)
to
(0,1)
brings a factor

\frac{\mathrm{w}_{\tau}}{2\mathrm{w}_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}}=\frac{2e^{2U}\sinh(2J)}{2(e^{2(U-J)}-1)},

by definition of the measure: closing
e
in
\omega_{\tau}
creates a cluster in
\omega_{\tau}
, hence the factor
2
in the denominator, and swaps the value of
(\omega_{\tau}(e),\omega_{\tau\tau}^{\prime}(e))
from
(1,1)
, which has weight
\mathrm{w}_{\tau}
, to
(0,1)
, which has weight
\mathrm{w}_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}
. The claim follows from the definition of
\mathrm{w}_{\tau},\mathrm{w}_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}
, see (
7
), the fact that there are
2(M+1)
edges of the form
\mathbf{b}_{e},\mathbf{f}_{e}
(
2
per regular cone-point,
M+1
regular cone-points), and that the event obtained from
\{\mathcal{C}=\eta_{L}\sqcup\eta_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\eta_{M}\sqcup\eta_{R}\}
by closing the edges of the form
\mathbf{b}_{e},\mathbf{f}_{e}
is precisely
A_{w_{L}}(\tilde{\eta}_{L})\cap A_{w_{1}}(\tilde{\eta}_{1})\cap\dots\cap A_{w_{R}}(\tilde{\eta}_{R})
.
∎

For
\tilde{\eta}_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
, and
\tilde{\eta}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}\cup\mathfrak{B}_{R}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
, introduce the conditional weights

(39)

\begin{gathered}q_{L}(\tilde{\eta}_{L}):=\Phi\big(A_{w_{L}}(\tilde{\eta}_{L})\big),\\
q(\tilde{\eta}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{L},\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k}):=c_{J,U}\Phi\big(A_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\eta})}(\tilde{\eta})\,\big|\,A_{w_{L}}(\tilde{\eta}_{L}),A_{w_{1}}(\tilde{\eta}_{1}),\dots,A_{w_{k}}(\tilde{\eta}_{k})\big),\end{gathered}

where
\bar{\eta}=\tilde{\eta}_{L}\circ\tilde{\eta}_{1}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{k}
.
By Claim
7.11
, we get the following decomposition:

(40)

\Phi(\mathcal{C}=\tilde{\eta}_{L}\circ\tilde{\eta}_{1}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{M}\circ\tilde{\eta}_{R})\\
=q_{L}(\tilde{\eta}_{L})q(\tilde{\eta}_{1}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{L})q(\tilde{\eta}_{2}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{L},\tilde{\eta}_{1})\dots q(\tilde{\eta}_{M}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{L},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{M-1}).

Let us describe informally the second part of the proof.
So far the probability of a given cluster linking
0
to
x
is decomposed as a product of conditional kernels (which are
not
probability kernels). The idea is now to represent this as a mixture of
independent
(factorized) kernels defined on diamond-confined graphs.
Then we will suitably normalize them by a factor
e^{t_{0}\cdot\mathrm{X}(\gamma)}
to obtain probability kernels.

7.7.
Proof of Theorem
7.1
part II: Mixing of weights and renewal structure

We will use the same procedure as
[
AOV24
, Section 7]
with the tricks from
[
OV18
, Appendix C]
to compensate for the lack of monotonicity in the conditional kernels. We only describe the needed inputs, and will refer to
[
AOV24
, Section 7]
once we arrive at a stage where the remaining arguments is a copy-pasting of
[
AOV24
, Section 7]
.

The first step is to prove that the conditional kernels of (
40
) have good mixing properties. This is the content of the next lemma.

Lemma 7.12
.

With the notations and definitions of Subsection
7.6
, there exist
\rho>0,C\geq 0,c>0
, such that

•

for every
\tilde{\eta}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}\cup\mathfrak{B}_{R}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
, one has that

(41)

\inf_{n\geq 0}\inf_{\tilde{\eta}_{0}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}}\inf_{\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{n}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}}q(\tilde{\eta}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{0},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{n})\geq\rho^{|\tilde{\eta}|};

•

for any
n,k,k^{\prime}\geq 0
, any
\tilde{\eta}_{0},\tilde{\eta}_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
, any
\tilde{\eta}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}\cup\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
, and any
\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k+n},\tilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k^{\prime}}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}

(42)

\Big|\frac{q\big(\tilde{\eta}\,\big|\,\tilde{\eta}_{0},\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k},\tilde{\eta}_{k+1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k+n}\big)}{q\big(\tilde{\eta}\,\big|\,\tilde{\eta}_{0}^{\prime},\tilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime},\tilde{\eta}_{k+1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k+n}\big)}-1\Big|\leq Ce^{-cn}.

Proof.

The first point is by definition of
q
, see (
39
), and finite energy for
\Phi
: the support of the event
A_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\eta})}(\tilde{\eta})
in (
39
) is of size at most
4|\tilde{\eta}|
. Focus on the second point. We implicitly always work in a large finite volume,
\Lambda_{N}=\{-N,\dots,N\}^{2}
, with
0,1
boundary conditions and take limits (everything being uniform over the large enough volume). Let
\tilde{\eta}_{k^{\prime}+i}^{\prime}\equiv\tilde{\eta}_{k+i}
for
i=1,\dots n
. For
l\geq 0
, let

v_{l}=\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{l}),\quad v_{l}^{\prime}=\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{l}^{\prime}),\quad w_{l}=\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{0}\circ\tilde{\eta}_{1}\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{l}),\quad w_{l}^{\prime}=\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{0}^{\prime}\circ\tilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime}\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{l}^{\prime}).

Let also

u_{l}=w_{k+l}-w_{k}=\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{k+1}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{k+l})=w_{k^{\prime}+l}^{\prime}-w_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}.

For
v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, introduce the translations of the events corresponding to a given chain of irreducible graphs:

\displaystyle B_{L,v}=A_{v}(\tilde{\eta}_{0})\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{k}A_{v+w_{i-1}}(\tilde{\eta}_{i}),\quad B_{L,v}^{\prime}=A_{v}(\tilde{\eta}_{0}^{\prime})\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}A_{v+w_{i-1}^{\prime}}(\tilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle B_{v}=\bigcap_{l=1}^{n}A_{v+u_{l}}(\tilde{\eta}_{k+l}),\quad B_{R,v}=A_{v+u_{n}}(\tilde{\eta}).

Now, that by definition of
q
(see (
39
)),

\frac{q\big(\tilde{\eta}\,\big|\,\tilde{\eta}_{0},\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k},\tilde{\eta}_{k+1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k+n}\big)}{q\big(\tilde{\eta}\,\big|\,\tilde{\eta}_{0}^{\prime},\tilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime},\tilde{\eta}_{k+1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k+n}\big)}\\
=\frac{\Phi\big(A_{w_{k+n}}(\tilde{\eta})\,\big|\,A_{0}(\tilde{\eta}_{0}),A_{w_{0}}(\tilde{\eta}_{1}),\dots,A_{w_{k-1}}(\tilde{\eta}_{k})\big)}{\Phi\big(A_{w_{k^{\prime}+n}^{\prime}}(\tilde{\eta})\,\big|\,A_{0}(\tilde{\eta}_{0}),A_{w_{0}^{\prime}}(\tilde{\eta}_{1}),\dots,A_{w_{n+k^{\prime}-1}^{\prime}}(\tilde{\eta}_{n+k^{\prime}}^{\prime})\big)}=\frac{\Phi\big(B_{R,w_{k}}\,\big|\,B_{L,0}\cap B_{w_{k}}\big)}{\Phi\big(B_{R,w_{k}^{\prime}}\,\big|\,B_{L,0}^{\prime}\cap B_{w_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}}\big)}.

Then, using translation invariance of
\Phi
,

(43)

\frac{\Phi\big(B_{R,w_{k}}\,\big|\,B_{L,0}\cap B_{w_{k}}\big)}{\Phi\big(B_{R,w_{k}^{\prime}}\,\big|\,B_{L,0}^{\prime}\cap B_{w_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}}\big)}=\frac{\Phi\big(B_{R,0}\,\big|\,B_{L,-w_{k}}\cap B_{0}\big)}{\Phi\big(B_{R,0}\,\big|\,B_{L,-w_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{\prime}\cap B_{0}\big)}\\
=\frac{\Phi\big(B_{R,0}\cap B_{L,-w_{k}}\,\big|\,B_{0}\big)}{\Phi\big(B_{R,0}\,\big|\,B_{0}\big)\Phi\big(B_{L,-w_{k}}\,\big|\,B_{0}\big)}\frac{\Phi\big(B_{R,0}\,\big|\,B_{0}\big)\Phi\big(B_{L,-w_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{\prime}\,\big|\,B_{0}\big)}{\Phi\big(B_{L,-w_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{\prime}\cap B_{R,0}\,\big|\,B_{0}\big)}.

Now,
B_{R,0}
is supported on the edges with at least one endpoint in
u_{n}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}
, denoted
E_{\triangleleft}(u_{n})
, whilst
B_{L,-w_{k}},B_{L,-w_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{\prime}
are supported on the edges with at least one endpoint in
-\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}
, denoted
E_{\triangleright}
. Looking at (
43
), (
42
) will follow from

(1)

a uniform upper and lower bounds on (
43
),

(2)

a suitable form of ratio mixing for
P(\cdot):=\Phi(\cdot\,|\,B_{0})
.

Indeed, (2) deals with the case
n
large enough, while small values of
n
are covered by (1). We start with (1).

Claim 7.13
.

There exists
c\geq 1
such that for any
n\geq 0
, any
v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, any
\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{n}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}

with
\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{1}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{n})=v
, any
F_{\triangleright}\subset E_{\triangleright}
,
F_{\triangleleft}\subset E_{\triangleleft}(v)
finite, and any
\alpha\in\{(0,0),(0,1),(1,1)\}^{F_{\triangleleft}}
,

\frac{1}{c}\leq\inf_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\frac{P(Y_{F_{\triangleleft}}=\alpha\,|\,Y_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi)}{P(Y_{F_{\triangleleft}}=\alpha\,|\,Y_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi^{\prime})}\leq\sup_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\frac{P(Y_{F_{\triangleleft}}=\alpha\,|\,Y_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi)}{P(Y_{F_{\triangleleft}}=\alpha\,|\,Y_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi^{\prime})}\leq c,

where the sup/inf are over
\xi,\xi^{\prime}
having positive probability,
P=\Phi(\ \,|\,B_{0})
, and
Y_{F}=(\omega_{\tau}|_{F},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}|_{F})
.

Proof.

By symmetry of the expression, it is sufficient to prove the upper bound. Let
M\geq 1
be a large enough integer and
\Lambda_{M}=\{-M,\dots,M\}^{2}
,
E_{M}=\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{M}}
. Let
\xi,\xi^{\prime}
have positive
P
-probability.
We now considering a decomposition

\displaystyle B_{0}=D_{B0}\cap D_{BR},\quad\{Y_{F_{\triangleleft}}=\alpha\}=D_{R0}\cap D_{R},

\displaystyle\{Y_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi\}=D_{L}\cap D_{L0},\quad\{Y_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi^{\prime}\}=D_{L}^{\prime}\cap D_{L0}^{\prime},

where the events
D_{L}
and
D_{L}^{\prime}
are supported on
E_{\triangleright}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{M}}
, the events
D_{RB}
and
D_{R}
are supported on
E_{\triangleleft}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{M}}
, and the events
D_{L0}
,
D_{L0}^{\prime}
,
D_{B0}
, and
D_{R0}
are supported on
\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{M}}
.

Now, by finite energy applied to lower bound the probability of the events with supported in
\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{M}}
conditionally on the others, one has that for some
c>0
,

\frac{P(Y_{F_{\triangleleft}}=\alpha\,|\,Y_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi)}{P(Y_{F_{\triangleleft}}=\alpha\,|\,Y_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi^{\prime})}\\
=\frac{\Phi(D_{R0}\cap D_{R}\cap D_{L0}\cap D_{L}\cap D_{B0}\cap D_{BR})\Phi(D_{L0}^{\prime}\cap D_{L}^{\prime}\cap D_{B0}\cap D_{BR})}{\Phi(D_{L0}\cap D_{L}\cap D_{B0}\cap D_{BR})\Phi(D_{R0}\cap D_{R}\cap D_{L0}^{\prime}\cap D_{L}^{\prime}\cap D_{B0}\cap D_{BR})}\\
\leq\frac{\Phi(D_{R}\cap D_{L}\cap D_{BR})\Phi(D_{L}^{\prime}\cap D_{BR})}{\Phi(D_{L}\cap D_{BR})\Phi(D_{R}\cap D_{L}^{\prime}\cap D_{BR})}e^{cM^{2}}.

But, by exponential ratio weak mixing (Theorem
3.3
), for
M
large enough depending only on the angular aperture of
\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}
,

\frac{\Phi(D_{L}\cap D_{R}\cap D_{BR})}{\Phi(D_{L}^{\prime}\cap D_{R}\cap D_{BR})}\leq\frac{\Phi(D_{L})\Phi(D_{R}\cap D_{BR})}{\Phi(D_{L}^{\prime})\Phi(D_{R}\cap D_{BR})}\frac{1+e^{-cM}}{1-e^{-cM}}\leq 2\frac{\Phi(D_{L})}{\Phi(D_{L}^{\prime})},

and

\frac{\Phi(D_{L}^{\prime}\cap D_{BR})}{\Phi(D_{L}\cap D_{BR})}\leq\frac{\Phi(D_{L}^{\prime})\Phi(D_{BR})}{\Phi(D_{L})\Phi(D_{BR})}\frac{1+e^{-cM}}{1-e^{-cM}}\leq 2\frac{\Phi(D_{L}^{\prime})}{\Phi(D_{L})}.

This concludes the proof of the claim.
∎

Divide the proof of ratio mixing into two claims. We start by proving a mixing bound for
P
. We will use the following observation: let
\gamma
be the simple closed dual path surrounding the connected graph
\tilde{\eta}_{1}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{n}
.
Let
*\gamma
be the set of primal edges that are crossed by
\gamma
, and let
V_{\gamma}
be the set of sites surrounded by
\gamma
. Then,

(44)

P|_{\mathbb{E}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{V_{\gamma}}}=\mathtt{ATRC}_{\mathbb{E}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{V_{\gamma}}}^{1,1}\big(\ \,\big|\,\omega_{\tau}|_{*\gamma}=0\big)=\mathtt{ATRC}_{\mathbb{E}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{V_{\gamma}}}^{0,1}\big(\ \,\big|\,\omega_{\tau}|_{*\gamma}=0\big),

where
\omega_{\tau}|_{*\gamma}
is the restriction of
\omega_{\tau}
to
*\gamma
, see Fig.
21
.

Figure 21.
Black edges have state
(1,1)
, gold ones have state
(0,1)
, thin lines are dual to edges where
\omega_{\tau}
is
0
. Left: the percolation event one conditions on. Right: the boundary conditions it effectively imposes.

For
\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{n}
,
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
as before, let
E_{\epsilon,\times}(\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{n})
be the edges with both endpoints in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\big((u_{\lceil n/3\rceil}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright}_{t_{0},\delta+\epsilon})\cup(u_{\lfloor 2n/3\rfloor}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}_{t_{0},\delta+\epsilon})\big)
, where
t_{0},\delta
are fixed in (
38
).

Claim 7.14
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, there exist
n_{0}\geq 1
,
C\geq 0,c>0
such that, for any
n\geq n_{0}
, any
\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{n}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
, any finite sets of edges
F_{\triangleright}\subset E_{\triangleright}
,
F_{\triangleleft}\subset E_{\triangleleft}
,
F_{\times}\subset E_{\epsilon,\times}(\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{n})
, any
F_{1}\in\{F_{\triangleright},F_{\triangleleft},F_{\times}\}
, and any
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in\{(0,0),(0,1),(1,1)\}^{F_{2}}
, where
F_{2}=(F_{\triangleright}\cup F_{\triangleleft}\cup F_{\times})\setminus F
,

\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}\big(P(X_{F_{1}}\in\cdot\,|\,X_{F_{2}}=\xi),P(X_{F_{1}}\in\cdot\,|\,X_{F_{2}}=\xi^{\prime})\big)\leq Ce^{-cn},

where
P,X
are defined as in Claim
7.13
.

Proof.

The idea is simple but the details of the percolation estimate are a bit tedious to write down, so we only sketch the percolation argument. The formal implementation is similar to
[
OV18
, Lemma 3.2]
. Looking at (
44
),
P|_{\mathbb{E}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{V_{\gamma}}}
has the
l
-path decoupling property of Section
6.1
. One can therefore use Theorem
6.1
to obtain the claim. Indeed, one can bound the wanted total variation distance with a connection probability in a Bernoulli block percolation model. The model one compares with is at worse of the type depicted on Fig.
22
.

Figure 22.
The grey grid represent the blocks of the block-percolation of Theorem
6.1
. The orange squares have a strictly smaller than one probability to be open. The white squares have a probability as small as wanted to be open. The red regions represent
E_{\triangleright},E_{\epsilon,\times},E_{\triangleleft}
.

The
n/3
cone-points between the different regions each provides an opportunity to disconnect
F_{1}
from
F_{2}
: each induces a bottleneck in the orange chain illustrated on Fig.
23
, and each bottleneck gives a positive probability to disconnect
F_{1}
from
F_{2}
. The choice of the larger angular opening for the central region (in Figure
22
),
\delta+\epsilon
instead of
\delta
, is a simple way to get rid of connections between the left/right red regions of Figure
22
and the central one occurring very high/low in the
\mathrm{e}_{2}
-direction.

Figure 23.
The grey grid represent the blocks of the block-percolation of Theorem
6.1
. The orange squares have a strictly smaller than one probability to be open. The light blue squares have a probability as small as wanted to be open. By exponential decay in the blue blocks, a positive density of bottleneck have the property that the two orange sides of the blue square are not connected in the blue blocks. Then, with positive probability, the blocks in the square are closed, cutting the orange chain at that bottleneck.

∎

We then turn this into ratio mixing using Appendix
B.1
.

Claim 7.15
.

There exists
C\geq 0,c>0,n_{0}\geq 1
such that for any
n\geq n_{0}
, any
v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, any
\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{n}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}

with
\mathrm{X}(\tilde{\eta}_{1}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{\eta}_{n})=v
, any
F_{\triangleright}\subset E_{\triangleright}
,
F_{\triangleleft}\subset E_{\triangleleft}(v)
finite, and any
\alpha\in\{(0,0),(0,1),(1,1)\}^{F_{\triangleleft}}
,

\sup_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\Big|\frac{P(X_{F_{\triangleleft}}=\alpha\,|\,X_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi)}{P(X_{F_{\triangleleft}}=\alpha\,|\,X_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi^{\prime})}-1\Big|\leq Ce^{-cn},

where the sup is over
\xi,\xi^{\prime}
having positive probability,
P,X
are defined as in Claims
7.13
, and
7.14
.

Proof.

The proof will be an application of Lemma
B.1
. Recall (
44
).

Let
1-\delta>\epsilon>0
, and let
E_{\times}=E_{\epsilon,\times}(\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{n})
. We apply Lemma
B.1
with

•

\Omega_{1}=\{(0,0),(0,1),(1,1)\}^{F_{\triangleleft}}
,
\Omega_{2}=\{(0,0),(0,1),(1,1)\}^{E_{\times}}
,

•

\mu=P(\ \,|\,X_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi)|_{F_{\triangleleft}\sqcup E_{\times}}
,
\nu=P(\ \,|\,X_{F_{\triangleright}}=\xi^{\prime})|_{F_{\triangleleft}\sqcup E_{\times}}
,

•

D\subset\Omega_{2}
is the event that there is an open path in
\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}
from the top boundary of
\Lambda_{N}
(the implicit finite volume we work in) to its bottom boundary staying in
E_{\times}
, and that there is a dual path of open edges in
\omega_{\tau}^{*}
from the top boundary of
\Lambda_{N}
to its bottom boundary using only edges dual to edges in
E_{\times}
.

The hypotheses of Lemma
B.1
hold with
\epsilon=e^{-cn}
when
n
is large enough by Claim
7.14
, and the uniform exponential decay of
\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}^{*},\omega_{\tau}
(Theorem
3.5
).
∎

This concludes the proof of (
42
).
∎

Lemma
7.12
is the needed input to put us in the framework of
[
AOV24
, Section 7]
. Let us describe the needed modifications which all take place in the beginning of the argument. The main difference is that our conditional weights
q
are not monotonic. We thus need to define the
p_{k}
’s used there in a slightly different way.

For
k>l\geq 1
,
\tilde{\eta}_{0}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
,
\tilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k-1}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
, and
\tilde{\eta}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}\cup\mathfrak{B}_{R}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}
, set

\displaystyle a_{0}(\tilde{\eta}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{0},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k-1})\equiv a_{0}(\tilde{\eta})=\inf_{n\geq 0}\inf_{\zeta_{0}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}}\inf_{\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{n}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}}q\big(\tilde{\eta}\,\big|\,\zeta_{0},\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{n}\big),

\displaystyle a_{k}(\tilde{\eta}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{0},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k-1})=q(\tilde{\eta}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{0},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k-1}),

and

a_{l}(\tilde{\eta}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{0},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k-1})\equiv a_{l}(\tilde{\eta}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{k-l},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k-1})\\
=\inf_{n\geq 0}\inf_{\zeta_{0}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}}\inf_{\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{n}\in\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle ir}}}q\big(\tilde{\eta}\,\big|\,\zeta_{0},\dots,\zeta_{n},\tilde{\eta}_{k-l},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k-1}\big)

In worlds:
a_{k}
records the minimal mass given by a conditional probability to an irreducible graph for a fixed frozen “recent” past, and any “less recent” possible past. Introduce then the mass increments (
p_{k}
has the same definition domain as
a_{k}
)

p_{0}=a_{0},\quad p_{k}=a_{k}-a_{k-1}.

Note that by definition of the
a_{k}
’s, the
p_{k}
’s are always non-negative. This property is what compensates the lack of property P6: monotonicity of
q(\tilde{\eta}\,|\,\tilde{\eta}_{0},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k-1})
. One can now duplicate
[
AOV24
, Section 7]
to conclude the proof of Theorem
7.1
with the following adaptations: use the
p_{k}
’s defined above in place of the ones defined in
[
AOV24
]
, and use Lemma
7.12
as a replacement for
[
AOV24
, properties P5, P7]
. Indeed, these are the only properties used in the argument.

8.
Invariance principle for the modified ATRC cluster

8.1.
Notations and main result of the section

Recall
\mathtt{mATRC}_{n,m}(\ \,|\,v_{L}\leftrightarrow v_{R})
defined in Section
4
.
As in Section
7
, we will describe the geometry of the cluster of
v_{L}
under
\mathtt{mATRC}_{n,m}(\ \,|\,v_{L}\leftrightarrow v_{R})
using a coupling with a random walk bridge in such a way that the cluster is included in diamonds with endpoints at the random walk steps.
In this section, we will need to track certain constants that will play the role of minimal/maximal scale at which some estimates hold. Constants like
C,c,c^{\prime}
will be defined only locally, whilst numbered constants will have fixed value re-used in several places. All constants can depend on
J,U
but never on
n
.

To be able to re-use our result in future work
[
DGO26
]
, we state a slightly more general result than what we need here. The reader can think of
\Phi_{n}
as
\mathtt{mATRC}_{n/2,m_{n}}
. Moreover, for notational convenience, we translated everything so that
v_{L}=0
. In the setup below, one can think of
v_{n}
as
v_{R}-v_{L}
. Let
\mathcal{R}_{n}
be a sequence of rectangles of the form:

\mathcal{R}_{n}=\{0,\dots,n\}\times\{-m_{n}^{\prime},\dots,m_{n}\}

where
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}
are non-decreasing sequences whose properties are part of the main theorem. Also introduce

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{n}

\displaystyle=\{-1,\dots,n+1\}\times\{-m_{n}^{\prime}-1,\dots,m_{n}+1\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n}^{{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\{1,\dots,n-1\}\times\{1-m_{n}^{\prime},\dots,m_{n}-1\}.

Finally, introduce the family of rectangles: for
k,l\geq 0
,
n\geq 2k
,

(45)

\mathrm{rect}_{k,l}^{n}=\{k,\dots,n-k\}\times\{-l,\dots,l\}.

We will be considering families of measures
\Phi_{n}
with the following properties:

(I)

\Phi_{n}
has support contained in
(\{0,1\}^{2})^{\mathrm{E}_{n}}
with
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}\subset\mathrm{E}_{n}\subset\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{n}}
.

(II)

\Phi_{n}
is FKG-lattice.

(III)

\Phi_{n}(\cdot\,|\,1_{\mathrm{E}_{n}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}})
restricted to
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}
is equal to
\mathtt{ATRC}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}}^{1,1}
, and
\Phi_{n}(\cdot\,|\,0_{\mathrm{E}_{n}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}})
restricted to
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}
is equal to
\mathtt{ATRC}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}}^{0,0}
, where
1_{F}
and
0_{F}
are the events that all edges in
F
have state
(1,1)
and
(0,0)
respectively.
In particular, as
\Phi_{n}
is FKG-lattice,

(46)

\mathtt{ATRC}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}}^{0,0}\preccurlyeq\Phi_{n}|_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}}\preccurlyeq\mathtt{ATRC}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}}^{1,1}

(IV)

\Phi_{n}
has the following finite energy property: there exists
\alpha\in(0,1)
, independent of
n
such that, for any
e\in\mathrm{E}_{n}
,

(47)

\begin{gathered}\Phi_{n}((\omega_{\tau}(e),\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}(e))=(1,1)\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{E}_{n}\setminus e})\geq\alpha\ \Phi_{n}\text{-almost surely},\\
\Phi_{n}((\omega_{\tau}(e),\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}(e))=(0,0)\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{E}_{n}\setminus e})\geq\alpha\ \Phi_{n}\text{-almost surely},\end{gathered}

where
(\omega_{\tau},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}})\sim\Phi_{n}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{F}
is the sigma-algebra generated by
(\omega_{\tau}(e),\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}(e))_{e\in F}
.

(V)

\Phi_{n}
has the double-edge-state-swap property: there is
c_{\mathrm{swap}}>0
such that for any
n
, any
e=\{x,y\},f=\{i,j\}\in\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}
, and any configurations
a,b\in\{0,1\}^{\mathrm{E}_{n}}
with
\Phi_{n}(\{(a,b)\})>0
such that
i\in\mathcal{C}_{x}(a_{ef})\subset\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}\setminus\partial^{\mathrm{in}}\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}
,
j,y\notin\mathcal{C}_{x}(a_{ef})
,

(48)

\frac{\Phi_{n}(\{(a^{ef},b)\})}{\Phi_{n}(\{(a_{ef},b)\})}=c_{\mathrm{swap}},

where
a_{ef}(e^{\prime})=a^{ef}(e^{\prime})=a(e^{\prime})
if
e^{\prime}\notin\{e,f\}
, and
a_{ef}(e)=a_{ef}(f)=0
,
a^{ef}(e)=a^{ef}(f)=1
.

Note that by Hypotheses
(III)
,
\Phi_{n}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\mathtt{ATRC}_{J,U}
the unique infinite volume ATRC measure as soon as
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\infty
, as we work with
J,U
on the self dual curve, where there is a unique ATRC measure. We therefore denote

\Phi=\mathtt{ATRC}_{J,U}.

Let finally
v_{n}
be a sequence of points such that:

(49)

\norm{v_n-(n,0)}_{\infty}\leq 2,\quad\Phi_{n}(0\xleftrightarrow{\omega_{\tau}}v_{n})>0.

Let
(\omega_{\tau},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}})\sim\Phi_{n}
. We will be studying the cluster of
0
in
\omega_{\tau}
under
\Phi_{n}(\cdot\,|\,0\xleftrightarrow{\omega_{\tau}}v_{n})
, so we will use the shorter notations

\mathcal{C}_{x}=\text{ connected component of }x\text{ in }\omega_{\tau},\quad\mathcal{C}\equiv\mathcal{C}_{0}.

We will use the probabilistic description of long clusters from Theorem
7.1
with a suitable choice of cones. First, note that by reflection symmetry the (unique by Theorem
7.1
)
t\in\partial\mathcal{W}
dual to
\mathrm{e}_{1}
is proportional to
\mathrm{e}_{1}
. Moreover, it satisfies

(50)

t\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}=\nu(\mathrm{e}_{1})\equiv\nu_{1}.

So the only
t\in\partial\mathcal{W}
dual to
\mathrm{e}_{1}
is
\nu_{1}\mathrm{e}_{1}
. Then, by strict convexity and symmetry, the cones
\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}_{\nu_{1}\mathrm{e}_{1},\delta}
are invariant under reflection through
\{x:\ x_{2}=0\}
, and have an angular aperture continuous in
\delta
, strictly increasing, which converges to
0
as
\delta\to 0
and to
\pi
as
\delta\to 1
.
For
\theta\in(0,\pi/2)
, define the symmetric cones with angular aperture
2\theta
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}_{\theta}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\ \tan(\theta)x_{1}\geq|x_{2}|\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright}_{\theta}=-\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}_{\theta}.

Then, for any
\theta\in(0,\pi/2)
, there is a unique
\delta\in(0,1)
such that
\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}_{\theta}=\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}_{\nu_{1}\mathrm{e}_{1},\delta}
.
As in Section
7
, we say that
x
is a
\theta
-cone-point of
A
if
A\subset x+(\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}_{\theta}\cup\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright}_{\theta})
, and write

\displaystyle\mathrm{Diamond}_{\theta}(u,v)

\displaystyle=(u+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}_{\theta})\cap(v+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright}_{\theta}),

\displaystyle\textnormal{CPts}_{\theta}(A)

\displaystyle=\{x\in A:\ x\text{ is a }\theta\text{-cone-point of }A\}.

We will mostly use the value
\theta=\pi/4
, but we will need the value
\pi/8
for some intermediate results. We therefore set
\theta=\pi/4
by default, and will explicitly specify the use of different values.

\text{When omitted from the notation, }\theta\text{ is set to be }\pi/4.

Denote
\mathfrak{B}_{L},\mathfrak{B}_{R},\mathfrak{A}
the sets of backward-confined, forward-confined, diamond-confined graphs (see Section
7.1
) for
\theta=\pi/4
. Let then
p_{L},p_{R},p
be the measures given by Theorem
7.1
for
(\delta,\nu_{1}\mathrm{e}_{1})
with
\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}=\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}_{\nu_{1}\mathrm{e}_{1},\delta}
. Denote
\mathrm{OZ}=p^{\mathbb{N}}
the random chain probability measure,
\mathrm{OZwalk}=(p\circ\mathrm{X}^{-1})^{\otimes\mathbb{N}}
the random walk increment probability measure. Denote
\mathrm{OZDec}
the positive measure (
not
probability measure) on pairs length + sequences of graphs given by

(51)

\mathrm{OZDec}(M;\gamma_{L},\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{M},\gamma_{R})=\mathrm{C}p_{L}(\gamma_{L})p_{R}(\gamma_{R})\prod_{i=1}^{M}p(\gamma_{i}),

where
\mathrm{C}
is the constant given by Theorem
7.1
. Integrating with respect to
\mathrm{OZDec}
, we get

(52)

\int f(M;\gamma_{L},\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{M},\gamma_{R})\,d\mathrm{OZDec}\\
=\mathrm{C}\sum_{M\geq 0}\sum_{\gamma_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}}\sum_{\gamma_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{M}\in\mathfrak{A}}f(M;\gamma_{L},\dots,\gamma_{R})p_{L}(\gamma_{L})p_{R}(\gamma_{R})\prod_{i=1}^{M}p(\gamma_{i}).

In particular, the above is just a finite sum whenever
f
is supported on sequences of graphs with a bounded displacement of
\gamma_{L}\circ\gamma_{1}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{M}\circ\gamma_{R}
.
Let
(\gamma_{i})_{i\geq 1}\sim\mathrm{OZ}
. For any
v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, define

(53)

T_{v}=\inf\{k\geq 1:\ \sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{i})=v\},

where the inf of an empty set is set to be
+\infty
by convention. Let
\mathrm{OZ}_{v}=\mathrm{OZ}(\cdot\,|\,T_{v}<\infty)
, and
\mathrm{OZwalk}_{v}=\mathrm{OZwalk}(\cdot\,|\,T_{v}<\infty)
.
Note that
T_{v}
depends only on the sequence of displacements, it is thus also well defined under
\mathrm{OZwalk}
.
We will frequently use the following facts about
\mathrm{OZwalk}
without further details.

(1)

First, there exist
c_{-},c_{+}>0
such that for any
n\geq 1
,
v=(v_{1},v_{2})
with
n\leq v_{1}\leq 2n
,
|v_{2}|\leq\sqrt{n}
,

\frac{c_{-}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\mathrm{OZ}(T_{v}<\infty)\leq\frac{c_{+}}{\sqrt{n}}.

This follows from the same computation as the OZ asymptotics of Theorem
3.6
, see for example
[
AOV24
, Section 8.1]
.

(2)

Second, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exist
c>0,L_{0}
such that for any
k\geq 1
,
L\geq L_{0}
,

\mathrm{OZ}\Big(\sum_{i=k}^{k+L-1}\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{i})\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}\notin[(\mu_{1}-\epsilon)L,(\mu_{1}+\epsilon)L]\Big)\leq e^{-cL},

where
\mu_{1}=\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{A}}p(\gamma)\mathrm{X}(\gamma)\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}>0
. This is a standard large deviation estimate for sums of independent random variables with exponential tails.

(3)

Finally, a direct consequence of the two points above is that there exist
n_{0}\geq 1
,
c_{0},c,\rho>0
, such that for any
n\geq n_{0}
,
v=(n,v_{2})
with
|v_{2}|\leq\sqrt{n}
, and any
n\geq k_{n}\geq c_{0}\ln(n)
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{OZ}_{v}\big(\exists i\in\{1,\dots,n-k_{n}\}:\,|\{j:\,i\leq\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{1}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{j})\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}\leq i+k_{n}\}|<\rho k_{n}\big)\leq e^{-ck_{n}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{OZ}_{v}\big(\exists i\in\{1,\dots,T_{v}\}:\,|\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{i})|\geq k_{n}\big)\leq e^{-ck_{n}}.

Note that the first estimate is stronger than the second, but we state the second explicitly for clarity.

The following “representation as a mixture of random walk bridges” is the main result of this section.

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
0<J<U
satisfy
\sinh 2J=e^{-2U}
. Suppose
(\Phi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a family of measures satisfying Hypotheses
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
, and
(III)
.
Then, there exist
c>0,C_{0}\geq 0,c_{0}\geq 0,c_{1}\geq 0
such that the following holds: for any increasing sequence
\frac{\sqrt{n}}{c_{0}}\geq\mathrm{sc}_{n}\geq c_{0}\ln(n)
, there is
n_{0}\geq 1
such that for any
n\geq n_{0}
,
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq C_{0}n
, there exists a probability measure
\mathrm{MixMe}_{n}
supported on pairs
(\zeta_{L},\zeta_{R})\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}\times\mathfrak{B}_{R}
with displacement sup-norm at most
c_{1}\mathrm{sc}_{n}^{2}
such that, for
v(\zeta_{L},\zeta_{R}):=v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\zeta_{R})-\mathrm{X}(\zeta_{L})
and any
f
function of
\mathcal{C}
,

\Big|\sum_{\zeta_{L},\zeta_{R}}\mathrm{MixMe}_{n}(\zeta_{L},\zeta_{R})\mathrm{OZ}_{v(\zeta_{L},\zeta_{R})}\big[f(\zeta_{L}\circ\gamma_{1}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{M}\circ\zeta_{R})\big]\\
-\Phi_{n}\big[f(\mathcal{C})\,\big|\,v_{n}\in\mathcal{C}\big]\Big|\leq\norm{f}_{\infty}e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}.

Note that this is a total variation distance bound, so by existence of maximal coupling, this theorem gives the existence of a coupling between the law of
\mathcal{C}
under
\Phi_{n}\big(\penalty\ \cdot\penalty\ \,\big|\,v_{n}\in\mathcal{C}\big)
and a product law on chains of diamond confined graphs, such that
\mathcal{C}
coincides with the chain with a probability going to
1
as
n\to\infty
. The law of the sequence of cone-points of this chain is then a random walk bridge, and the distance (e.g. Hausdorff) between the linear interpolation of this bridge and the actual cluster is at most a constant times the norm of the largest step.
By a general result of Kovchegov
[
Kov04
]
, this linear interpolation converges to a Brownian Bridge under diffusive scaling and the maximal step size is
C\ln^{2}(n)
with probability going to
1
as
n\to\infty
(see Lemma
8.11
). This therefore implies an invariance principle for the upper and lower envelopes of
\mathcal{C}
.

8.2.
Implications for
\mathtt{mATRC}

In this paper, we are ultimately interested in a particular application of Theorem
8.1
. We apply it to

•

m_{n}=m_{n}^{\prime}=Cn
for
C
a large enough integer,

•

\Phi_{2n}
is the translate of
\mathtt{mATRC}_{n,m_{n}}
by
v_{L}
,

•

\mathrm{E}_{2n}=\bar{E}_{n,m_{n}}-v_{L}
is the translation of the augmented rectangular domain of Section
2
, see also Figure
24
,

•

v_{2n}=v_{R}-v_{L}
.

Figure 24.
\mathrm{E}_{n}
in the application to
\mathtt{mATRC}
.

Hypotheses
(III)
and
(III)
follow from Lemma
3.1
and the explicit expression for the density of
\mathtt{mATRC}_{n,m_{n}}
, see Definition
4.3
. Hypotheses
(III)
follows from Lemma
4.7
. Hypotheses
(III)
and
(III)
follow from the explicit expression for the density of
\mathtt{mATRC}_{n,m_{n}}
, see again Definition
4.3
.

8.3.
Good clusters and overlook of the proof

As the proof of Theorem
8.1
is fairly long and spans over the rest of the section, we start by introducing the main ideas of the proof. The first step is to prove that under
\Phi_{n}\big(\penalty\ \cdot\penalty\ \,\big|\,v_{n}\in\mathcal{C}\big)
, the cluster
\mathcal{C}
has a nice structure with very high probability. Let us describe what we mean by nice. Introduce

\mathcal{S}_{l,r}=[l,r]\times\mathbb{R},\quad\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\theta,l,r}(C)=\mathcal{S}_{l,r}\cap\textnormal{CPts}_{\theta}(C),\quad\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{l,r}\equiv\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/4,l,r}(C).

We introduce the set of
good clusters
:
C\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
if all of the following are fulfilled for some small enough
\rho>0
depending only on
U,J
:

(1)

v_{n}\in C
;

(2)

|\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{2,\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}(C)|\geq\rho\mathrm{sc}_{n}
,
|\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{n-\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,n-2}(C)|\geq\rho\mathrm{sc}_{n}
;

(3)

|\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,2\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}(C)|>0
,
|\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{n-2\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,n-\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}(C)|>0
;

(4)

|\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{2\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,3\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}(C)|\geq\rho\mathrm{sc}_{n}
,
|\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{n-3\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,n-2\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}(C)|\geq\rho\mathrm{sc}_{n}
;

(5)

|\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{3\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,4\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}(C)|>0
,
|\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{n-4\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,n-3\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}(C)|>0
.

The value of
\rho
is given by Lemmas
8.5
, and
8.6
.
We introduce the following notation (see Figure
25
):

•

if
\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{3\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,4\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}(\mathcal{C})\neq\varnothing
, then denote its leftmost point by
W_{L}
, and otherwise set
W_{L}=\dagger
;

•

if
\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{n-4\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,n-3\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}(\mathcal{C})\neq\varnothing
, then denote its rightmost point by
W_{R}
, and otherwise set
W_{R}=\dagger
;

•

\mathcal{K}_{L}:=\mathcal{C}\cap(W_{L}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright})
if
W_{L}\neq\dagger
, and
\mathcal{K}_{L}=\dagger
otherwise;

•

\mathcal{K}_{R}:=-W_{R}+\mathcal{C}\cap(W_{R}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright})
if
W_{R}\neq\dagger
, and
\mathcal{K}_{R}=\dagger
otherwise;

•

\mathcal{K}:=-W_{L}+\mathcal{C}\cap\mathrm{Diamond}(W_{L},W_{R})
if
W_{L}\neq\dagger
and
W_{R}\neq\dagger
, and
\mathcal{K}=\dagger
otherwise.

In particular, on the event
\{W_{L}\neq\dagger,W_{R}\neq\dagger\}
,
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{K}_{L}\circ\mathcal{K}\circ\mathcal{K}_{R}
.

The first step of the proof of Theorem
8.1
is to show that
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
with high probability; this is the content of Section
8.5
.
Then, on the event
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
, we will do a sampling as follows: first sample
\mathcal{K}_{L},\mathcal{K}_{R}
compatible with
\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
. Then, sample
\mathcal{K}
conditionally on
\mathcal{K}_{L},\mathcal{K}_{R}
. Mixing properties of
\Phi_{n}
, proved in Section
8.4
, will then allow us to show that for these realizations of
\mathcal{K}_{L},\mathcal{K}_{R}
, the density of
\mathcal{K}
conditional on
\mathcal{K}_{L},\mathcal{K}_{R}
is close to the density of an infinite volume cluster connecting
0
to
v_{n}
under
\mathtt{ATRC}
conditional on
\mathcal{K}_{L},\mathcal{K}_{R}
. This is done in Section
8.6
. But this last object is naturally coupled to a random walk bridge by Theorem
7.1
, which will allow us to conclude the proof of Theorem
8.1
in Section
8.7
. Section
8.8
then recalls the setup needed to go from Theorem
8.1
to the invariance principle.

Figure 25.
If
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
, the blue regions all contain at least
\rho\mathrm{sc}_{n}
cone-points. The green region represents
\Delta(W_{L},W_{R})
.

Finally, introduce a few additional objects that will be needed for the control of the conditional densities of
\mathcal{K}
. For
u,v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
with
u_{1}<v_{1}
, let, see Figure
25
,

(54)

\Delta(u,v)=\{u_{1},\dots,v_{1}\}\\
\times\{\min(u_{2},v_{2})-(v_{1}-u_{1}+2),\dots,\max(u_{2},v_{2})+(v_{1}-u_{1}+2)\}.

We will then need the
envelope
of cone-confined graphs. The
outer boundary
and
filling
of
V\subset\mathbb{Z}^{2}
finite connected (for nearest-neighbour connectivity) are, respectively, the set of edges
\mathrm{OutBnd}(V)
, the set of sites
\mathrm{Fill}(V)
obtained as follows: let
A_{0},\dots,A_{m}
be the connected components of
\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus V
with
A_{0}
the only infinite connected component. Then,
\mathrm{OutBnd}(V)=\partial^{\mathrm{edge}}A_{0}
, and
\mathrm{Fill}(V)=\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus A_{0}
. Define then the envelopes.

•

For
\gamma_{L}
a connected graph,
v\in\gamma_{L}
a vertex with
\gamma_{L}\subset v+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright}
, define

\mathrm{Env}_{L}(\gamma_{L})=\mathrm{OutBnd}(\gamma_{L}\setminus v).

Note that
v
is uniquely defined when it exists.

•

For
\gamma_{R}
a connected graph,
v\in\gamma_{R}
a vertex with
\gamma_{R}\subset v+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}
, define

\mathrm{Env}_{R}(\gamma_{R})=\mathrm{OutBnd}(\gamma_{R}\setminus v)

Note that
v
is uniquely defined when it exists.

•

For
\gamma
a connected graph,
u,v\in\gamma
with
\gamma\subset\mathrm{Diamond}(u,v)
, define

\displaystyle\mathrm{Env}(\gamma)=\mathrm{OutBnd}(\gamma\setminus\{u,v\}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Env}^{+}(\gamma)=\{e\in\mathrm{Env}(\gamma):\ e\text{ strictly above }p_{\gamma}\},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Env}^{-}(\gamma)=\{e\in\mathrm{Env}(\gamma):\ e\text{ strictly below }p_{\gamma}\},

where
p_{\gamma}
is any bi-infinite self-avoiding path obtained by gluing
(\dots,u-2\mathrm{e}_{1},u-\mathrm{e}_{1},u)
, any self-avoiding path from
u
to
v
inside
\gamma
, and
(v,v+\mathrm{e}_{1},v+\mathrm{e}_{2},\dots)
. In particular,
\mathrm{Env}^{+}(\gamma)\cup\mathrm{Env}^{-}(\gamma)=\mathrm{Env}(\gamma)\setminus\{\{u,u+\mathrm{e}_{1}\},\{v,v-\mathrm{e}_{1}\}\}
.

Then, introduce the sets of good forward, backward, and diamond contained marked graphs that can be realizations of
\mathcal{K}_{L}
,
\mathcal{K}_{R}
, and
\mathcal{K}
respectively. For
v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,

(55)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{L,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})

\displaystyle=\big\{(\gamma_{L},0):\ 0\in\gamma_{L},\ \widetilde{\Phi}_{n}\big(W_{L}=\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{L}),\,\mathcal{K}_{L}=\gamma_{L}\big)>0\big\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{R,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})

\displaystyle=\big\{(\gamma_{R},v):\ v\in\gamma_{R},\ \widetilde{\Phi}_{n}\big(W_{R}=v_{n}-v,\,\mathcal{K}_{R}=\gamma_{R}\big)>0\big\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}_{v}

\displaystyle=\big\{\gamma\in\mathfrak{A}:\ \mathrm{X}(\gamma)=v\big\},

where
\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}=\Phi_{n}(\,\cdot\,\,|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n}))
.
Finally, introduce the events linked with the occurrence of cone confined graphs.
These are finite volume versions of the events
A_{v}(\eta)
introduced in Section
7.6
. For
F\subset\mathbb{E}
,
w\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, define the following:

•

For
0\in\gamma_{L}
,
\gamma_{L}
connected such that
\gamma_{L}\subset v+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright}
for a (uniquely defined)
v\in\gamma_{L}
, let
A_{w,F}^{L}(\gamma_{L})
be the event

A_{w,F}^{L}(\gamma_{L})=\bigcap_{e\in\gamma_{L}\cap F}\big\{\omega_{\tau}(w+e)=\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}(w+e)=1\big\}\cap\bigcap_{e\in(\partial^{\mathrm{edge}}\gamma_{L}\cap F)\setminus\{v,v+\mathrm{e}_{1}\}}\big\{\omega_{\tau}(w+e)=0\big\}.

•

For
0,v\in\gamma_{R}
,
\gamma_{R}
connected such that
\gamma_{R}\subset\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}
, let
A_{w,F}^{R}(\gamma_{R})
be the event

A_{w,F}^{R}(\gamma_{R})=\bigcap_{e\in\gamma_{R}\cap F}\big\{\omega_{\tau}(w+e)=\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}(w+e)=1\big\}\cap\bigcap_{e\in(\partial^{\mathrm{edge}}\gamma_{R}\cap F)\setminus\{0,-\mathrm{e}_{1}\}}\big\{\omega_{\tau}(w+e)=0\big\}.

•

For
0,v\in\gamma
,
\gamma
connected such that
\gamma\subset\mathrm{Diamond}(0,v)
, let
A_{w,F}^{0}(\gamma)
be the event

A_{w,F}^{0}(\gamma)=\bigcap_{e\in\gamma\cap F}\big\{\omega_{\tau}(w+e)=\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}(w+e)=1\big\}\cap\bigcap_{e\in(\partial^{\mathrm{edge}}\gamma\cap F)\setminus\mathrm{bnd}_{\gamma}}\big\{\omega_{\tau}(w+e)=0\big\},

where
\mathrm{bnd}_{\gamma}=\big\{\{0,-\mathrm{e}_{1}\},\{0,\mathrm{e}_{2}\},\{0,-\mathrm{e}_{2}\},\{v,v+\mathrm{e}_{1}\},\{v,v+\mathrm{e}_{2}\},\{v,v-\mathrm{e}_{2}\}\big\}
.

When
F
is omitted from the notation, it is set to be
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}
.

8.4.
Basic properties of the measure

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
0<J<U
satisfy
\sinh 2J=e^{-2U}
. Suppose the sequence
\Phi_{n}
satisfies Hypotheses
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
, and
(III)
. Then, there exists
c>0
depending on
\alpha,J,U
such that for any
n,m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq 1
, any simply connected
F\subset\mathrm{E}_{n}
, and any
x,y\in\mathbb{V}_{F}
,

(56)

\sup_{\eta}\Phi_{n}\big(x\xleftrightarrow{F}y\,\big|\,\omega_{\tau}(e)=\eta(e)\,\forall e\in F^{c}\big)\leq e^{-c|x-y|}.

In particular, there exist integers
n_{0}\geq 1,\geq 0
such that, for any
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n
, and
n\geq n_{0}
,

(57)

\Phi_{n}(\exists x\in\mathcal{C}:\ |x_{2}|\geq\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n)\leq e^{-3\nu_{1}n}.

Proof.

Note that (
57
) follows from (
56
) and a union bound: taking
\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}\geq\frac{100}{c}\nu_{1}
, we have (for
n
large enough)

\Phi_{n}(\exists x\in\mathcal{C}:\ |x_{2}|\geq\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n)\leq\sum_{x:\norm{x}_{\infty}\geq\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}e^{-c|x|}\leq\sum_{k\geq\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}4ke^{-ck/2}\leq e^{-25\nu_{1}n}.

Thus, we focus on (
56
).
By Hypotheses
(III)
, for any
F\subset\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{n}}
,

\Phi_{n}\big(\penalty\ \cdot\penalty\ \,\big|\,\omega_{\tau}(e)=\eta(e)\,\forall e\in F^{c}\big)\preccurlyeq\mathtt{ATRC}_{F}^{1,1}=\Phi(\penalty\ \cdot\penalty\ \,|\,\omega_{\tau}(e)=1\,\forall e\in F^{c}).

The right-hand side decays exponentially by Theorem
3.5
, thus implying (
56
) whenever
F\subset\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{n}}
.
To extend (
56
) to the case
F\not\subset\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{n}}
, we use one-dimensionality of
\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{n}}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{n}}
and the finite energy property of
\Phi_{n}
(Hypotheses
(III)
).
We only sketch the argument. One can first sample the configuration on
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{n}}
. By the previous observation, all clusters there have exponential tails. Then, the edges in
\mathrm{E}_{n}\cap(\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{n}}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{n}})
do not allow to merge many small clusters into something much bigger because each of these edges is closed with a uniformly positive probability.
This was made formal in a classical work of Alexander
[
Ale04
]
.
Though our setting does not fit in
[
Ale04
, Theorem 1.1]
, the arguments in
[
Ale04
, Section 2]
do apply
mutatis mutandis
.
∎

Using Theorem
3.3
, we obtain a fast relaxation of
\Phi_{n}
towards
\Phi
.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
0<J<U
satisfy
\sinh 2J=e^{-2U}
. Suppose the sequence
\Phi_{n}
satisfies Hypotheses
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
, and
(III)
.
Then, there exist
c>0,C\geq 0
such that, for every
n,m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq 2
, any
F\subset\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}
, and any event
A
supported on
F
with
\Phi(A)>0
,

\Big|\frac{\Phi_{m}(A)}{\Phi(A)}-1\Big|\leq C\sum_{e\in F}e^{-c\mathrm{d}_{\infty}(e,(\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime})^{c})},

as soon as the R.H.S. is strictly less than
0.1
. In particular, there exists
\geq 0
such that, for any
a>0
,
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq an+\Cr{cst:minMixScale}\ln(n)
, one can find
n_{0}\geq 1
such that for any
n\geq n_{0}
and any event
B
supported on
\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{rect}_{\Cr{cst:minMixScale}\ln(n),an}^{n}}
, one has

\tfrac{1}{2}\leq\frac{\Phi_{n}(B)}{\Phi(B)}\leq 2.

Proof.

This follows from Hypotheses
(III)
, the exponential ratio weak mixing of
\mathtt{ATRC}
(Theorem
3.3
), and Lemma
C.1
: by Theorem
3.3
, for any
A
supported on
F
,

\Big|\frac{\mathtt{ATRC}^{1,1}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}}(A)}{\mathtt{ATRC}^{0,0}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}}(A)}-1\Big|\leq C\sum_{e\in F}\sum_{e^{\prime}\in\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime}}^{c}}e^{-c\mathrm{d}_{\infty}(e,e^{\prime})}\leq C^{\prime}\sum_{e\in F}e^{-c\mathrm{d}_{\infty}(e,(\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime})^{c})}.

For
F=\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{rect}_{\Cr{cst:minMixScale}\ln(n),an}^{n}}
, the last sum can then be made as small as wanted by taking
\mathrm{rect}_{\Cr{cst:minMixScale}\ln(n),an}^{n}
at distance at least
c^{\prime}\ln(n)
from
(\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime})^{c}
for
c^{\prime}
large enough, which amounts to take
\Cr{cst:minMixScale}
large enough.
The claim then follows from Lemma
C.1
(
0.1
is any small enough constant to control the ratios in the conclusion of Lemma
C.1
).
∎

Our last “preparatory Lemma” is a sharp-up-to-constant lower bound on the probability of the event on which we want to condition.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
0<J<U
satisfy
\sinh 2J=e^{-2U}
. Suppose the sequence
\Phi_{n}
satisfies Hypotheses
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
, and
(III)
.
Then, there exists
c\geq 0
such that, for any
a>0
, the following holds.
There exists
n_{0}\geq 1
such that, for any
n\geq n_{0}
,
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq an+\Cr{cst:minMixScale}\ln(n)
,

\Phi_{n}(v_{n}\in\mathcal{C})\geq\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}e^{-n\nu_{1}}.

Proof.

Let
x_{N}=(N,0)
,
y_{N}=(n-N,0)
with
N
to be fixed large enough later. Let

V_{N}=\mathbb{Z}^{2}\cap[N,n-N]\times[-an,an]\cap(x_{N}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft})\cap(y_{N}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright}),\quad F_{N}=\mathbb{E}_{V_{N}}.

Then, by inclusion of events and FKG inequality,

(58)

\displaystyle\Phi_{n}(v_{n}\in\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\geq\Phi_{n}(0\leftrightarrow x_{2N})\Phi_{n}(v_{n}\leftrightarrow y_{2N})\Phi_{n}(x_{2N}\xleftrightarrow{F_{N}}y_{2N})

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-C\sum_{e\in F_{N}}e^{-c\mathrm{d}_{\infty}(e,(\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime})^{c})}\Big)\alpha^{4N}\Phi(x_{2N}\xleftrightarrow{F_{N}}y_{2N}),

where we used Lemma
8.3
, and Hypotheses
(III)
in the second line. Now, choosing
N
large enough as a function of
C
and
c
, we get
C\sum_{e\in F_{N}}e^{-c\mathrm{d}_{\infty}(e,(\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\prime})^{c})}\leq\frac{1}{2}
for any
n
large enough.

To conclude, we can then use Theorem
7.1
to lower bound the remaining probability. Let
x_{N}=S_{0},S_{1},S_{2},\dots
be a random walk with step distribution
\mathrm{OZwalk}
. Using the finite energy of the probability measures given by Theorem
7.1
,
\Phi(x_{N}\xleftrightarrow{F_{N}}y_{N})
is greater than
C^{\prime}e^{-\nu_{1}(n-4N)}
times

\mathrm{OZwalk}\Big(T_{y_{2N}-x_{2N}}<\infty,\ \bigcup_{i=1}^{T_{y_{2N}-x_{2N}}}\mathrm{Diamond}(S_{i-1},S_{i})\\
\subset[N,n-N]\times[-an,an]\cap\big(x_{N}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}\big)\cap\big(y_{N}+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright}\big)\Big),

where
S_{0}=x_{N}
,
S_{i}=X_{i}+S_{i-1}
,
(X_{i})_{i\geq 1}\sim\mathrm{OZwalk}
, and
T
is
defined in (
53
). The steps
X_{i}
’s are supported on the whole of
\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, have exponential tails, and mean proportional to
\mathrm{e}_{1}
.
Then, the Local Limit Theorem applies and a simple computation implies that the probability of
T_{y_{2N}-x_{2N}}<\infty
is greater than
C/\sqrt{n}
; see eg.
[
AOV24
]
between eq.
(20)
and
(22)
. Adding the constraint that the diamond envelope has the wanted containment property only costs a multiplicative constant. This is a straightforward consequence of the exponential tails of the steps. The reader interested by the precise implementation of this last point can have a look at
[
OV19
, Lemmas 2.6,2.7]
for the implementation of a very similar (though more involved) claim.
∎

8.5.
Typical geometry

We will go through an intermediate set of edges before trying to control
\mathcal{C}
. Introduce
\omega_{\tau}|_{k,l}^{n}\equiv\omega_{\tau}|_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{rect}_{k,l}^{n}}}
the restriction of
\omega_{\tau}
to the edges with both endpoints in
\mathrm{rect}_{k,l}^{n}
, and the set of edges in
\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{rect}_{k,l}}
that are connected to a left-right crossing of
\mathrm{rect}_{k,l}
:

(59)

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k,l}\equiv\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k,l,n}(\omega_{\tau})=\big\{e\in\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{rect}_{k,l}^{n}}:\ \omega_{\tau}(e)=1,\ e\xleftrightarrow{\omega_{\tau}|_{k,l}^{n}}\{k\}\times\{-l,\dots,l\},\\
e\xleftrightarrow{\omega_{\tau}|_{k,l}^{n}}\{n-k\}\times\{-l,\dots,l\}\big\}.

Also, introduce the event
\mathrm{DoubleCross}_{k,l}
that
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k,l}
contains at least two disjoint paths from
\{k\}\times\mathbb{Z}
to
\{n-k\}\times\mathbb{Z}
.

To get control over the geometry of
\mathcal{C}
under
\Phi_{n}(\cdot\,|\,v_{n}\in\mathcal{C})
, we will first get control over the geometry of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}
and then prove that
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}
are actually very close.

The next Lemma is our only use (in Section
8
) of an angular aperture different than
\pi/4
for cone-points. The angle is therefore explicitly mentioned in the notation.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
0<J<U
satisfy
\sinh 2J=e^{-2U}
. Suppose the sequence
\Phi_{n}
satisfies Hypotheses
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
, and
(III)
. Let
\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}
be given by Lemma
8.2
, and
\Cr{cst:minMixScale}
be given by Lemma
8.3
.
Then, there exist
n_{0}\geq 1
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
\rho>0
,
K_{0}\geq 0
,
c>0,C\geq 0
such that for any
n\geq n_{0}
,
\epsilon_{0}n\geq k_{n}\geq\Cr{cst:minMixScale}\ln(n)
,
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq 2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n+k_{n}
, one has the following bounds.

(1)

\Phi_{n}\big(\mathrm{DoubleCross}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\big)\leq e^{-\nu_{1}n-cn}
.

(2)

\Phi_{n}\big(\varnothing\neq\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\not\subset\mathcal{C},\ v_{n}\in\mathcal{C}\big)\leq e^{-\nu_{1}n-cn}
.

(3)

For any
K\geq K_{0}
,

\Phi_{n}\big(\varnothing\neq\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\subset\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}},\ \exists x\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{E}_{n}}:\ x\leftrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n},\\
\mathrm{d}_{\infty}\big(x,\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\cap\{k_{n},n-k_{n}\}\times\mathbb{Z}\big)\geq Kk_{n}\big)\leq e^{-\nu_{1}n-cKk_{n}}.

(4)

For any
K\geq K_{0}
, and any
i\in\{k_{n},\dots,n-(K+1)k_{n}\}
,

\Phi_{n}\big(\varnothing\neq\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\subset\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}},\ |\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/8,i,i+Kk_{n}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n})|<\rho Kk_{n}\big)\leq e^{-\nu_{1}n-cKk_{n}}.

Proof.

Proceed in increasing order.

Start with item
1
.

The proof goes as follows: if there are at least two crossings of
\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}
, one can condition on the lowest. Then, the probability of a second crossing given the lowest one is at most
(4\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n)^{2}e^{-c(n-2k_{n})}
uniformly over the lowest crossing by a union bound and Theorem
3.5
. Indeed, one can dominate the measure conditioned on the lowest crossing by an ATRC measure on the part of
\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}
above the lowest crossing with
1,1
boundary conditions. The later has exponential decay of connectivities by Theorem
3.5
. Re-summing over the lowest crossing, we obtain as upper bound
(4\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n)^{2}e^{-c(n-2k_{n})}
times the probability that
\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}
is crossed. Using Lemma
8.3
, a union bound, and Theorem
7.1
, one can upper bound this probability by
2(4\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n)^{2}e^{-\nu_{1}(n-2k_{n})}
for any
n
large enough. This gives the first point once
\epsilon_{0}>0
is taken small enough and
n_{0}
large enough.

Continue with item
2
.

Assume
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\neq\varnothing
and
v_{n}\in\mathcal{C}
.
If
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\not\subset\mathcal{C}
, then at least one of the two following events occurs:

•

There exists
x\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{E}_{n}}
with
|x_{2}|\geq\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n
such that
x\in\mathcal{C}
.

•

\mathrm{DoubleCross}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
occurs.

The first event has probability at most
e^{-3\nu_{1}n}
by Lemma
8.2
, and the second has probability at most
e^{-\nu_{1}n-cn}
by item
1
.

Turn to item
3
.

By item
1
, we can restrict to the case were the event
\mathrm{DoubleCross}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
does not occur. In particular, we can assume that
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
is connected. Now, observe that, as
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\subset\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}}
, if
x
is connected to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
but is not part of it,
x
has to be connected to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\cap\{k_{n},n-k_{n}\}\times\{-\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n,\dots,\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n\}
, see Figure
26
.
Now, by Lemma
8.2
, the probability that
x
is connected to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
conditionally on
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
is at most
\exp(-c\rmd_{\infty}(x, \crossCl^{k_n,2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\cap\{k_n,n-k_n\}\times\Z))
. The claim follows from a union bound over
x
conditionally on
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
.

Finish with item
4
.

Under the event
\varnothing\neq\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\subset\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}}
, there exist
x\in\{k_{n}\}\times\{-\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n,\dots,\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n\}
, and
y\in\{n-k_{n}\}\times\{-\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n,\dots,\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n\}
such that
x
is connected to
y
in
\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}
. For
\epsilon>0
, let

A_{\epsilon}^{n}=\big\{\exists x,y\in\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}:\ x_{1}=k_{n},\>y_{1}=n-k_{n},\>|x_{2}-y_{2}|\geq\epsilon n,\>x\xleftrightarrow{\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}}y\big\}.

Now, by a union bound, Theorem
3.6
, and Lemma
8.3
, one has that for any
\epsilon>0
and
n
large enough,

\displaystyle\Phi_{n}\big(A_{\epsilon}^{n}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{x:x_{1}=k_{n},|x_{2}|\leq\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\sum_{y:y_{1}=n-k_{n},|y_{2}|\leq\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\mathds{1}_{|x_{2}-y_{2}|\geq\epsilon n}\Phi(x\leftrightarrow y)

\displaystyle\leq 4\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n\sum_{y:y_{1}=n-2k_{n},|y_{2}|\leq 2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\mathds{1}_{|y_{2}|\geq\epsilon n}e^{-\nu(y)}

\displaystyle\leq 4\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n\sum_{\epsilon n\leq|k|\leq 2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}e^{-\nu_{1}(n-2k_{n})-c|k|}\leq(4\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n)^{2}e^{-\nu_{1}(n-2k_{n})-c\epsilon n},

for some
c>0
as
\nu
is a uniformly convex norm. The above shows that we can fix any
\epsilon>0
and restrict the study of the wanted probability to
(A_{\epsilon}^{n})^{c}
at the price of taking
\epsilon_{0}
small enough. Now, take
\epsilon>0
small enough so that we can apply Theorem
7.1
with
s_{0}=\mathrm{e}_{1}
to connections between
x,y
with
x_{1}<y_{1}
,
|x_{2}-y_{2}|\leq\epsilon(y_{1}-x_{1})
.
Moreover, by item
1
, we can work under
(\mathrm{DoubleCross}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n})^{c}
, so we can assume
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
is connected.
Now, remains to study the event of item
4
under the event
(\mathrm{DoubleCross}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n})^{c}\cap(A_{\epsilon}^{n})^{c}
. In particular, that there are
x,y\in\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
with
x_{1}=k_{n}
,
y_{1}=n-k_{n}
,
|y_{2}-x_{2}|\leq\epsilon n
,
x
connected to
y
in
\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
, and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
is the cluster of
x,y
in
\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}
. We can thus make a union bound over such
x,y
’s, and use Lemma
8.3
to replace
\Phi_{n}
by
\Phi
to obtain that the probability of the intersection of the event in item
4
with
(\mathrm{DoubleCross}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n})^{c}\cap(A_{\epsilon}^{n})^{c}
is less or equal to

2\sum_{x,y}\Phi\big(y\in\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\mathrm{restr}},\ |\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/8,i,i+Kk_{n}}(\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\mathrm{restr}})|<\rho Kk_{n}\big)\\
\leq 2\sum_{x:x_{1}=n-2k_{n},|x_{2}|\leq\epsilon n}\Phi\big(x\in\mathcal{C},\ |\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/8,i,i+Kk_{n}}(\mathcal{C})|<\rho Kk_{n}\big)

where
i
is as in item
4
, the first sum is over
x,y
with
x_{1}=k_{n}
,
y_{1}=n-k_{n}
,
|x_{2}-y_{2}|\leq\epsilon n
,
|x_{2}|,|y_{2}|\leq\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n
, and
\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\mathrm{restr}}
is the cluster of
x
in the restriction of
\omega_{\tau}
to
\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{rect}_{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}^{n}}
. The inequality uses that, under the event
x\leftrightarrow y
,
\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/8,i,i+Kk_{n}}(\mathcal{C}_{x}^{\mathrm{restr}})\subset\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/8,i,i+Kk_{n}}(\mathcal{C}_{x})
, with
\mathcal{C}_{x}
the cluster of
x
, and translation invariance of
\Phi
. Now, using Theorem
7.1
, the exponential tails of the measures
p\circ\mathrm{X}^{-1}
,
p_{L}\circ\mathrm{X}^{-1}
,
p_{R}\circ\mathrm{X}^{-1}
there, and classical large deviation bounds for deviation of sums of i.i.d. random vectors with exponential tails away from their mean, one directly obtains that

\sum_{x:x_{1}=n-2k_{n},|x_{2}|\leq\epsilon n}\Phi\big(x\in\mathcal{C},\ |\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/8,i,i+Kk_{n}}(\mathcal{C})|<\rho Kk_{n}\big)\leq e^{-\nu_{1}(n-2k_{n})}e^{-cKk_{n}},

for some
c>0
, and all
n
large enough, as soon as
K
is larger than some fixed constant.
∎

Figure 26.
The open cluster
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}
(in black) and the edges forced to be closed by its presence (dual of the red). The blue points are where connections to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}
have to arrive.

We then push the properties of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}
to
\mathcal{C}
using a deterministic observation about cone-points.

Figure 27.
Red: the cone-point for
\theta=\pi/8
. Blue: the cone-point for
\theta=\pi/4
. The addition of the grey area is compensated by the increase of the cone opening.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
0<J<U
satisfy
\sinh 2J=e^{-2U}
. Suppose the sequence
\Phi_{n}
satisfies Hypotheses
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
, and
(III)
.
Then, there exist
n_{0}\geq 1
,
>0
,
\geq 5
,
c>0
, such that for any
n\geq n_{0}
,
\frac{n}{\Cr{cst:scaleGCl}}\geq\mathrm{sc}_{n}\geq\Cr{cst:scaleGCl}\ln(n)
,
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq\Cr{cst:scaleMinBox}n
, one has

\Phi_{n}\big(\mathcal{C}\notin\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n}),\ v_{n}\in\mathcal{C}\big)\leq e^{-\nu_{1}n-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}.

Proof.

Let
\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster},\Cr{cst:minMixScale}
be the constants given by Lemmas
8.2
, and
8.3
respectively. The deterministic observation is that for any
L\geq 1
, and
V
connected, any
x\in\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/8}(V)
also satisfies
x\in\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/4}(\langle V\rangle_{L})
, where
\langle V\rangle_{L}
is given by

\langle V\rangle_{L}=V\cup\bigcup_{y\in V:y_{1}<x_{1}-\frac{2}{2-\sqrt{2}}L}(y+[-L,L]^{2})\cup\bigcup_{y\in V:y_{1}>x_{1}+\frac{2}{2-\sqrt{2}}L}(y+[-L,L]^{2})

where we used
\tan(\pi/8)=\sqrt{2}-1
and
\tan(\pi/4)=1
. See Figure
27
. We will use Lemma
8.5
to say that
\mathcal{C}
is close to some crossing cluster (included in a slight enlargement), and that the said crossing cluster has all the cone-points required to be a good cluster,
but for a smaller cone opening
. Then, we increase the cone opening to take care of the slight enlargement of the crossing cluster needed to control
\mathcal{C}
.

Introduce

k_{n}=\lfloor\mathrm{sc}_{n}/r\rfloor.

for some
r>0
to be chosen large later.

Let

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{\partial}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\cap\{k_{n},n-k_{n}\}\times\mathbb{Z}.

Let
K,K^{\prime}\geq 1
be some integers to be fixed large later, and
\rho>0
is small to be fixed small later. Introduce the events

•

A_{1}(K)
is the event that
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\subset\mathcal{C}
and that

\mathcal{C}\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n}\cup\bigcup_{x\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{\partial}}(x+[-Kk_{n},Kk_{n}]^{2}).

•

A_{2}(\rho,K^{\prime})
is the event that for every
i\in\{k_{n},\dots,n-(K^{\prime}+1)k_{n}\}
,
|\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/8,i,i+K^{\prime}k_{n}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n})|\geq\rho k_{n}
.

By Lemma
8.5
, if we fix
\rho>0
small enough, there exist
c>0,K_{0}\geq 0,n_{0}\geq 0
such that for any
K,K^{\prime}\geq K_{0}
, and any
n\geq n_{0}
,

\Phi_{n}\big(A_{1}(K)^{c},\ v_{n}\in\mathcal{C}\big)\leq e^{-\nu_{1}n-cKk_{n}},\quad\Phi_{n}\big(A_{2}(\rho,K^{\prime})^{c},\ v_{n}\in\mathcal{C}\big)\leq e^{-\nu_{1}n-cK^{\prime}k_{n}},

as long as
\epsilon_{0}n\geq k_{n}\geq\Cr{cst:minMixScale}\ln(n)
with
\epsilon_{0}>0
fixed given by Lemma
8.5
, and
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq 2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n+k_{n}
.
Now, the deterministic observation implies that on the event
A_{1}(K)\cap\{v_{n}\in\mathcal{C}\}
,

\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/8,a_{K}k_{n},n-a_{K}k_{n}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{k_{n},2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n})\subset\mathcal{S}\textnormal{CPts}_{\pi/4,a_{K}k_{n},n-a_{K}k_{n}}(\mathcal{C})

where
a_{K}=\frac{2K}{2-\sqrt{2}}+1
. The conclusion follows once we fix
K,K^{\prime}
large enough, and take
r=10a_{K}
(
10
is an arbitrary number strictly larger than
2
), as the cone-points required to be in
\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
are included in those guaranteed by the occurrence of
A_{2}(\rho,K^{\prime})
. The constant
\Cr{cst:scaleGCl}
is taken large enough so that
k_{n}=\mathrm{sc}_{n}/r
can fit the required constraints, and the constant
\Cr{cst:scaleMinBox}
is taken to be any constant such that
\Cr{cst:scaleMinBox}n\geq 2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n+k_{n}=2\Cr{cst:maxHeightCluster}n+\mathrm{sc}_{n}/r
for all
n
large enough and all
\frac{n}{\Cr{cst:scaleGCl}}\geq\mathrm{sc}_{n}\geq\Cr{cst:scaleGCl}\ln(n)
.
∎

8.6.
Density swapping

We now study the density of
\Phi_{n}\circ\mathcal{C}^{-1}
on the event
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
for
\mathrm{sc}_{n}
as in Lemma
8.6
. Recall the notations and definitions introduced in Section
8.3
and the edge-set
\mathrm{E}_{n}
on which
\Phi_{n}
is defined.

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
0<J<U
satisfy
\sinh 2J=e^{-2U}
. Suppose the sequence
\Phi_{n}
satisfies Hypotheses
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
,
(III)
, and
(III)
. Let
\Cr{cst:scaleGCl},\Cr{cst:scaleMinBox}
be given by Lemma
8.6
. Then, there are
n_{0}\geq 1
,
c>0
, such that for any
n\geq n_{0}
,
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq\Cr{cst:scaleMinBox}n
,
\frac{n}{\Cr{cst:scaleGCl}}\geq\mathrm{sc}_{n}\geq\Cr{cst:scaleGCl}\ln(n)
, one has that for every
(\gamma_{L},0)\in\mathfrak{B}_{L,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
, every
(\gamma_{R},v)\in\mathfrak{B}_{R,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
, and every
\gamma
connected containing
0
and
v_{n}-v-u
, where
u\equiv\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{L})
, with
\gamma\subset\mathrm{Diamond}(0,v_{n}-v-u)
,

\Big|\frac{\Phi_{n}\big(A_{u}^{0}(\gamma)\,|\,A_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{v_{n}-v,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R}),\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})\big)}{\Phi\big(A_{u}^{0}(\gamma)\,|\,A_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{v_{n}-v}^{R}(\gamma_{R}),\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})\big)}-1\Big|\leq e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}.

Proof.

To shorten notations, let
w_{L}=\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{L})
,
w_{R}=v_{n}-v
. We will use several times that
A_{u}^{0}(\gamma)
is supported on edges with both endpoints in
\Delta(w_{L},w_{R})
.
First, notice that, as
(\gamma_{L},0)\in\mathfrak{B}_{L,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
, and
(\gamma_{R},v)\in\mathfrak{B}_{R,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
, there is a leftmost cone-point of
\gamma_{L}
in the slab
\mathcal{S}_{2,\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}
, denote it
w_{L}^{1}
, a leftmost cone-point of
\gamma_{L}
in the slab
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,2\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}
, denote it
w_{L}^{2}
, and a leftmost cone-point of
\gamma_{L}
in the slab
\mathcal{S}_{2\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,3\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}
, denote it
w_{L}^{3}
.
In the same fashion, there is a rightmost cone-point of
v_{n}-v+\gamma_{R}
in the slab
\mathcal{S}_{n-\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,n-2}
, denote it
w_{R}^{1}
, a rightmost cone-point of
v_{n}-v+\gamma_{R}
in the slab
\mathcal{S}_{n-2\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,n-\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}
, denote it
w_{R}^{2}
, and a rightmost cone-point of
v_{n}-v+\gamma_{R}
in the slab
\mathcal{S}_{n-3\mathrm{sc}_{n}+1,n-2\mathrm{sc}_{n}-1}
, denote it
w_{R}^{3}
. See Figure
28
.

Let then

F_{n}=\mathbb{E}_{\{w_{L}^{1}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1},\dots,w_{R}^{1}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}\}\times\{-5n,\dots,5n\}},

and introduce the sets, see Figure
28
,

E_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{\Delta(w_{L},w_{R})},\quad E_{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\{w_{L}^{2}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1},\dots,w_{R}^{2}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}\}\times\{-4n,\dots,4n\}\setminus\{w_{L}^{3}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1},\dots,w_{R}^{3}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}\}\times\{-3n,\dots,3n\}}.

Note that
\Delta(w_{L},w_{R})\subset\{w_{L}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1},\dots,w_{R}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}\}\times\{-2n,\dots,2n\}
by definition, and that
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq 5n
by choice of
\Cr{cst:scaleMinBox}
.

Introduce the following modifications
\tilde{A}_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R}),\,\tilde{A}_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})
of
A_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R}),\,A_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{w_{R}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})
: ask that
\{w_{L}^{1},w_{L}^{1}+\mathrm{e}_{1}\},\{w_{R}^{1},w_{R}^{1}-\mathrm{e}_{1}\}
are closed in
\omega_{\tau}
rather than open. See Figure
28
.

Figure 28.
For
\gamma_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
,
\gamma_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
,
\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}
both contain at least
\rho\mathrm{sc}_{n}
cone-points in the blue and white annuli. The thick blue edges are the ones which states are changed in
\tilde{A}_{v}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})
. The red paths are the edges dual to
\mathrm{Env}_{L}(\gamma_{L})
, and to
\mathrm{Env}_{R}(v_{n}-v+\gamma_{R})
. A zoom on the relevant part of the box is made, white strips represent parts of the system that are not represented.

We first observe that by Hypotheses
(III)
, and the same observation as in Claim
7.11
,

(60)

\displaystyle\frac{\Phi_{n}\big(A_{w_{L}}^{0}(\gamma),\,\tilde{A}_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}{\Phi_{n}\big(A_{u}^{0}(\gamma),\,A_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}

\displaystyle=c_{\mathrm{swap}},

\displaystyle\frac{\Phi\big(A_{u}^{0}(\gamma),\,\tilde{A}_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}{\Phi\big(A_{u}^{0}(\gamma),\,A_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{w_{R}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\sinh(2J)}{e^{-2J}-e^{-2U}}\Big)^{2}=:c_{J,U}.

Thus, as

\Phi_{n}\big(\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n}),\,A_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)\\
=\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{A}_{w_{R}-w_{L}}}\Phi_{n}\big(A_{w_{L}}^{0}(\gamma^{\prime}),\,A_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big),

we have that

(61)

\Phi_{n}\big(A_{w_{L}}^{0}(\gamma)\,\big|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n}),\,A_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)\\
=\frac{\Phi_{n}\big(A_{w_{L}}^{0}(\gamma)\,\big|\,\tilde{A}_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}{\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{A}_{w_{R}-w_{L}}}\Phi_{n}\big(A_{w_{L}}^{0}(\gamma^{\prime})\,\big|\,\tilde{A}_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)},

and similarly for
\Phi
. The Lemma will follow from the above observations and the next claim.

Claim 8.8
.

There exist
c>0
and
n_{0}\geq 1
such that, for any
n\geq n_{0}
and any event
B
supported on edges with both endpoints in
\Delta(w_{L},w_{R})
,

\Big|\frac{\Phi_{n}\big(B\,|\,\tilde{A}_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}{\Phi\big(B\,|\,\tilde{A}_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}-1\Big|\leq e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}.

Proof.

We use Lemma
B.1
with
\epsilon=e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}
and the following inputs:

•

\mu
is the restriction of
\Phi_{n}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)
to
E_{1}\cup E_{2}
,
\mu_{1}
is the restriction of
\mu
to
E_{1}
, and
\mu_{2}
is the restriction of
\mu
to
E_{2}
.

•

\nu
is the restriction of
\Phi\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)
to
E_{1}\cup E_{2}
,
\nu_{1}
is the restriction of
\nu
to
E_{1}
, and
\nu_{2}
is the restriction of
\nu
to
E_{2}
.

•

D
is the event that
E_{2}
contains a circuit of primal edges open in
\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}
, and a circuit of dual edges open in
\omega_{\tau}^{*}
which both surround
E_{1}
.

We need to show that the Hypotheses of Lemma
B.1
are fulfilled. Introduce the shorthands

\tilde{A}_{F}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\equiv\tilde{A}_{0,F}^{L}(\gamma_{L})\cap\tilde{A}_{w_{R},F}^{R}(\gamma_{R}),\quad\tilde{A}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\equiv\tilde{A}_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L})\cap\tilde{A}_{w_{R}}^{R}(\gamma_{R}).

Let

\tilde{\gamma}_{L}=\gamma_{L}\cap\mathrm{Diamond}(w_{L}^{1},w_{L}),\quad\tilde{\gamma}_{R}=(\gamma_{R}+w_{R})\cap\mathrm{Diamond}(w_{R},w_{R}^{1}),

and

\mathcal{E}=\mathrm{Env}^{+}(\tilde{\gamma}_{L})\cup\mathrm{Env}^{-}(\tilde{\gamma}_{L})\cup\mathrm{Env}^{+}(\tilde{\gamma}_{R})\cup\mathrm{Env}^{-}(\tilde{\gamma}_{R})\cup\big\{\{w_{L}^{1},w_{L}^{1}+\mathrm{e}_{1}\},\{w_{R}^{1},w_{R}^{1}-\mathrm{e}_{1}\}\big\}.

Let
N=10^{10^{m_{n}+m_{n}^{\prime}+n}}
and set

V_{N}=\{-N,\dots,N\}^{2}\setminus(\mathrm{Fill}(\tilde{\gamma}_{L})\cup\mathrm{Fill}(\tilde{\gamma}_{R})),\quad Q=\mathtt{ATRC}_{\mathbb{E}_{V_{N}}}^{1,1}\big(\cdot\,\big|\,\omega_{\tau}(e)=0\,\forall e\in\mathcal{E}\big).

As we will see below, any large enough integer as function of
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime},n
would do.

The proofs of Hypotheses 1 and 2 of Lemma
B.1
are very close to the proof of Claim
7.14
. We therefore refer to it after reducing the wanted claim to a statement analogous to the one present in the proof of Claim
7.14
.

Hypotheses 1 of Lemma
B.1
holds:

let
a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{E_{1}}
with
a\leq b
,
a^{\prime}\leq b^{\prime}
. Use the shorthand
F_{n}^{\prime}=F_{n}\setminus E_{1}
Then, let
\pi
be a monotone coupling of
\mathtt{ATRC}^{1,1}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)
, and
\mathtt{ATRC}^{0,0}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)
, and
(X,Y)\sim\pi
. Then, by Hypotheses
(III)
,
(III)
, the measures
\Phi_{n}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{\mathrm{E}_{n}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}),\,\omega_{\tau}|_{E_{1}}=a,\,\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}|_{E_{1}}=b\big)
,
\Phi\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}),\,\omega_{\tau}|_{E_{1}}=a,\,\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}|_{E_{1}}=b\big)
, as well as their versions with
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}
replacing
a,b
, are stochastically dominated by
\mathtt{ATRC}^{1,1}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)
, and stochastically dominate
\mathtt{ATRC}^{0,0}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)
. Thus, the total variation distance between
\Phi_{n}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{\mathrm{E}_{n}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}),\,\omega_{\tau}|_{E_{1}}=a,\,\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}|_{E_{1}}=b\big)
and
\Phi_{n}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{\mathrm{E}_{n}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}),\,\omega_{\tau}|_{E_{1}}=a^{\prime},\,\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}|_{E_{1}}=b^{\prime}\big)
is less than
\pi(X|_{E_{2}}\neq Y|_{E_{2}})
. The same holds for
\Phi
. Then, by a union bound an monotonicity of
\pi
,

\displaystyle\pi(X|_{E_{2}}\neq Y|_{E_{2}})
