\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in E_{2}}\Big(\mathtt{ATRC}^{1,1}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\omega_{\tau}(e)=1\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)-\mathtt{ATRC}^{0,0}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\omega_{\tau}(e)=1\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathtt{ATRC}^{1,1}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}(e)=1\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)-\mathtt{ATRC}^{0,0}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}(e)=1\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)\Big).

We then will use Theorem
6.1
to bound the last differences. Letting
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
be the union of
E_{1}
with the set of edges with at least one endpoint in
\{x\in\mathbb{V}_{F_{n}}^{c}:\ \mathrm{d}_{\infty}(x,\mathbb{V}_{F_{n}})=1\}
, we have that (recall that
Q=\mathtt{ATRC}_{\mathbb{E}_{V_{N}}}^{1,1}\big(\cdot\,\big|\,\omega_{\tau}(e)=0\,\forall e\in\mathcal{E}\big)
) for any event
B
supported on
E_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathtt{ATRC}^{1,1}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(B\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)=Q\big(B\,\big|\,\omega_{\tau}(e^{\prime})=\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}(e^{\prime})=1\,\forall e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\big),

\displaystyle\mathtt{ATRC}^{0,0}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(B\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)=Q\big(B\,\big|\,\omega_{\tau}(e^{\prime})=\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}(e^{\prime})=0\,\forall e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\big).

Theorem
6.1
can then be applied to
Q
to give that the differences are less than the probability in an inhomogeneous bloc percolation that
e
is connected to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, exactly as in the proof of Claim
7.14
. This probability is then less than
e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}
as both
\gamma_{L}
and
w_{R}+\gamma_{R}
contain at least
\rho\mathrm{sc}_{n}
cone points in the both slabs
\mathcal{S}_{w_{L}^{1}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1},w_{L}^{2}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}},\mathcal{S}_{w_{L}^{3}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1},w_{L}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{w_{R}^{2}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1},w_{R}^{1}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}},\mathcal{S}_{w_{R}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1},w_{R}^{3}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}}
respectively. The argument is then the same as in the proof of Claim
7.14
.

Hypotheses 2 of Lemma
B.1
holds:
let
\pi
be a monotone coupling of
\mathtt{ATRC}^{1,1}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)
, and
\mathtt{ATRC}^{0,0}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)
, and
(X,Y)\sim\pi
. Then, by Hypotheses
(III)
,
(III)
, the measures
\Phi_{n}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{\mathrm{E}_{n}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)
, and
\Phi\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)
are stochastically dominated by
\mathtt{ATRC}^{1,1}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)
, and stochastically dominate
\mathtt{ATRC}^{0,0}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})\big)
. Thus,
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu_{2},\nu_{2})\leq\pi(X|_{E_{2}}\neq Y|_{E_{2}})
. The rest is the same as in the previous point.

Hypotheses 3 of Lemma
B.1
holds:

let
(a,b)\in D
compatible with
\tilde{A}_{\mathrm{E}_{n}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R})
. We have that by Hypotheses
(III)
, Hypotheses
(III)
, and the lattice-FKG property of ATRC, the measures
\Phi_{n}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{\mathrm{E}_{n}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}),\,(\omega_{\tau},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}})|_{E_{2}}=(a,b)\big)
, and
\Phi\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}),\,(\omega_{\tau},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}})|_{E_{2}}=(a,b)\big)
are stochastically dominated by
\mathtt{ATRC}^{1,1}_{F_{n}}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}),\,(\omega_{\tau},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}})|_{E_{2}}=(a,b)\big)
, and stochastically dominate
\mathtt{ATRC}^{0,0}_{F_{n}}\big(\cdot\,|\,\tilde{A}_{F_{n}}(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}),\,(\omega_{\tau},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}})|_{E_{2}}=(a,b)\big)
. The path decoupling property of
\mathtt{ATRC}
, see Lemma
4.9
and the two paragraphs before it, imply that these two ATRC measures agree on
E_{1}
. So, the two measures restricted to
E_{1}
are equal, which is the “conditional equality” part of the third Hypotheses of Lemma
B.1
. The fact that
D
has probability at least
1-e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}
under
\mu_{2},\nu_{2}
follows from Lemma
8.2
for
\mu_{2}
, and Theorem
3.5
for
\nu_{2}
.
∎

By Claim
8.8
, and (
61
), we get, with
\epsilon_{n}\equiv e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{n}\big(A_{u}^{0}(\gamma)\,|\,A_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R}),\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\Phi_{n}\big(A_{w_{L}}^{0}(\gamma)\,|\,\tilde{A}_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}{\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{A}_{w_{R}-w_{L}}}\Phi_{n}\big(A_{w_{L}}^{0}(\gamma^{\prime})\,|\,\tilde{A}_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R},\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1+\epsilon_{n}}{1-\epsilon_{n}}\frac{\Phi\big(A_{w_{L}}^{0}(\gamma)\,|\,\tilde{A}_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}{\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{A}_{w_{R}-w_{L}}}\Phi\big(A_{w_{L}}^{0}(\gamma^{\prime})\,|\,\tilde{A}_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,\tilde{A}_{w_{R}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1+\epsilon_{n}}{1-\epsilon_{n}}\Phi\big(A_{u}^{0}(\gamma)\,|\,A_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{w_{R}}^{R}(\gamma_{R}),\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})\big),

which is half of the wanted claim. The other half follows the same computation with
\leq
replaced by
\geq
, and
\frac{1+\epsilon_{n}}{1-\epsilon_{n}}
replaced by
\frac{1-\epsilon_{n}}{1+\epsilon_{n}}
.
∎

From this Lemma and trivial algebra, we obtain the following: for
n
large enough,
\mathrm{sc}_{n}
as in Lemma
8.7
, one has that for any
A\subset\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
,

(62)

\displaystyle\Phi_{n}\big(\mathcal{C}\in A\,|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\lesseqgtr(1\pm e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}})\sum_{\gamma_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L,n}^{\mathrm{good}}}\sum_{\gamma_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R,n}^{\mathrm{good}}}\Phi_{n}\big(A_{0,\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{R}),\mathrm{E}_{n}}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\,\big|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\Phi\big(\mathcal{C}\in A\,\big|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n},\,A_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L}),\,A_{v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{R})}^{R}(\gamma_{R})\big)

where
\mathfrak{B}_{L,n}^{\mathrm{good}}\equiv\mathfrak{B}_{L,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
,
\mathfrak{B}_{R,n}^{\mathrm{good}}\equiv\mathfrak{B}_{R,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
, and
\mathrm{GCl}_{n}\equiv\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
.

8.7.
Proof of Theorem
8.1

The proof of Theorem
8.1
will build around (
62
), and the OZ decomposition of (
52
). For
\gamma_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}
,
\gamma_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R}
, and
C
a realization of
\mathcal{C}
under
\Phi
containing
0
and
v_{n}
, denote

•

C\sim\gamma_{L}
if
C\cap(\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{L})+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleright})=\gamma_{L}
and
\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{L})\in\textnormal{CPts}(C)
,

•

C\sim\gamma_{R}
if
C\cap(v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{R})+\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft})=\gamma_{R}
and
v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{R})\in\textnormal{CPts}(C)
.

Moreover, we will use the shorthand for sequences:

(x_{i},x_{i+1},\dots,x_{j})\equiv x_{i}^{j}.

For
\eta_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L},\,\eta_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R}
, introduce the probability measure
\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}
on the same space as
\mathrm{OZDec}
via: for any event
B
,

(63)

\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(B)\coloneqq\frac{\int d\mathrm{OZDec}(M;\gamma_{0}^{M+1})\mathds{1}_{\gamma_{0}^{M+1}\in B}\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\gamma})=v_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{L}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{R}}}{\int d\mathrm{OZDec}(M;\gamma_{0}^{M+1})\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\gamma})=v_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{L}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{R}}},

where
\mathrm{OZDec}
is the measure introduced in (
51
), and
where
\bar{\gamma}=\gamma_{0}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{M+1}
. Also, write

\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}\circ\bar{\gamma}^{-1}

the measure induced on connected subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
containing
0
by concatenation of the graph sequence sampled under
\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}
.
Then, note that for
\gamma_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
,
\gamma_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R,n}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
,

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})\}\cap A_{0}^{L}(\gamma_{L})\cap A_{v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{R})}^{R}(\gamma_{R})

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{A}_{v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{L})-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{R})}}\{\mathcal{C}=\gamma_{L}\circ\gamma\circ\gamma_{R}\}

\displaystyle=\big\{\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n}),\,\mathcal{C}\sim\gamma_{L},\,\mathcal{C}\sim\gamma_{R}\big\}.

Lemma 8.9
.

Let
0<J<U
satisfy
\sinh 2J=e^{-2U}
. Then, there exist
n_{0}\geq 1
,
c>0
, and
\epsilon_{0}>0
, such that for any
n\geq n_{0}
, and any
\eta_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L},\eta_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R}
with
\norm{\displace(\eta_L)}_{\infty},\norm{\displace(\eta_R)}_{\infty}\leq\epsilon_{0}\sqrt{n}
,

\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}\Big(\Phi\big(\mathcal{C}\in\cdot\,\big|\,\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n}),\,\mathcal{C}\sim\eta_{L},\,\mathcal{C}\sim\eta_{R}\big),\,\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}\circ\bar{\gamma}^{-1}\Big)\leq e^{-cn}.

Proof.

First, by Theorem
7.1
, there exists
c>0
such that for any
n
large enough, and any set
B
of realisations of
\mathcal{C}
,

(64)

\Big|e^{\nu_{1}v_{n}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}}\Phi\big(\mathcal{C}\in B,\,v_{n}\in\mathcal{C}\big)-\int d\mathrm{OZDec}(M;\gamma_{0}^{M+1})\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\gamma})=v_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\in B}\Big|\leq e^{-cn}

where
\bar{\gamma}=\gamma_{0}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{M+1}
. Then, by definition of
\mathrm{OZDec},\mathrm{OZwalk}
, see (
52
), and the “finite energy” properties of the probability measures
p_{L},p_{R},p
, see Theorem
7.1
, for some
c^{\prime}>0
, one has

\int d\mathrm{OZDec}(M;\gamma_{0}^{M+1})\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\gamma})=v_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{L}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{R}}\geq\mathrm{C}e^{-c^{\prime}(|\eta_{L}|+|\eta_{R}|)}\mathrm{OZwalk}(T_{v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\eta_{L})-\mathrm{X}(\eta_{R})}<\infty).

Finally, by a direct random walk computation using the Local Limit Theorem in dimension 2, see for example
[
AOV24
, Section 8.1]
, and the fact that
\norm{\displace(\eta_L)+\displace(\eta_R)}_{\infty}\leq 2\epsilon_{0}\sqrt{n}
,

\mathrm{OZwalk}(T_{v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\eta_{L})-\mathrm{X}(\eta_{R})}<\infty)\geq\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{n}},

for some
C>0
. Combining, we get, for
n
large enough,

\int d\mathrm{OZDec}(M;\gamma_{0}^{M+1})\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\gamma})=v_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{L}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{R}}\geq e^{-c^{\prime}\epsilon_{0}^{2}n}

for some
c^{\prime}>0
.
Now, by (
64
), for any
B
,

\Phi\big(\mathcal{C}\in B\,\big|\,\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n}),\,\mathcal{C}\sim\eta_{L},\,\mathcal{C}\sim\eta_{R}\big)\\
\lesseqgtr\frac{\pm e^{-cn}+\int d\mathrm{OZDec}(M;\gamma_{0}^{M+1})\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\in B}\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\gamma})=v_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{L}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{R}}}{\mp e^{-cn}+\int d\mathrm{OZDec}(M;\gamma_{0}^{M+1})\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\gamma})=v_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{L}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{R}}}.

So, if
\epsilon_{0}>0
is small enough, for any
n
large enough,

\displaystyle(1-e^{-cn/2})\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(B)-e^{-cn/2}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi\big(\mathcal{C}\in B\,\big|\,\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n}),\,\mathcal{C}\sim\eta_{L},\,\mathcal{C}\sim\eta_{R}\big)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-cn/2}+(1+e^{-cn/2})\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(B),

which is the wanted claim.
∎

For
\eta_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L},\eta_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R}
, and
\gamma_{0}^{M+1}\sim\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}
, define the random variables

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{\eta_{L}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\min\{k\geq 0:\ \eta_{L}\subset\gamma_{0}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{k}\}&\text{ if }\gamma_{0}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{M+1}\sim\eta_{L}\\
\dagger&\text{ else}\end{cases},

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{\eta_{R}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\max\{0\leq k\leq M+1:\ \eta_{R}\subset\gamma_{k}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{M+1}\}&\text{ if }\gamma_{0}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{M+1}\sim\eta_{R}\\
\dagger&\text{ else}\end{cases},

and the events for
K\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(K)=

\displaystyle\big\{\mathrm{T}_{\eta_{L}}\neq\dagger,\,\norm{\displace(\gamma_0\concatenate\dots\concatenate\gamma_{\rmT_{\eta_L}})}_{\infty}\leq K\big\}

\displaystyle\cap

\displaystyle\big\{\mathrm{T}_{\eta_{R}}\neq\dagger,\,\norm{\displace(\gamma_{\rmT_{\eta_R}}\concatenate\dots\concatenate\gamma_{M+1})}_{\infty}\leq K\big\}.

Now, for
K\geq\max(\norm{\displace(\eta_L)}_{\infty},\norm{\displace(\eta_R)}_{\infty})
, and
n
large enough, note that for any function
f

(65)

\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}\big(f(\bar{\gamma}_{0}^{M+1})\,;\,\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(K)\big)=\\
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L}\\
\norm{\displace(\gamma_L)}_{\infty}\leq K\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R}\\
\norm{\displace(\gamma_R)}_{\infty}\leq K\end{subarray}}\sum_{k,l\geq 0}\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}\big((\mathrm{T}_{\eta_{L}},\mathrm{T}_{\eta_{R}})=(k,l),\,\bar{\gamma}_{0}^{k}=\gamma_{L},\,\bar{\gamma}_{M+1-l}^{M+1}=\gamma_{R}\big)\\
\cdot\mathrm{OZ}_{v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{L})-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{R})}\big(f(\gamma_{L}\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{M}\circ\gamma_{R})\big),

where
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{M}\sim\mathrm{OZ}_{v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{L})-\mathrm{X}(\gamma_{R})}
, and
\bar{x}_{i}^{j}=x_{i}\circ\dots\circ x_{j}
. Note that, after conditioning on
\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(K)
, this is precisely a “bridge decomposition” for the probability measure
\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(\cdot\,|\,\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(K))
, which is what we are after. To conclude the proof of Theorem
8.1
, the idea is to prove that
\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(C\mathrm{sc}_{n}^{2})
has very large probability once
C
is taken large enough and
\mathrm{sc}_{n}
is as in the statement of Theorem
8.1
. This is the content of the next Lemma.

Lemma 8.10
.

Let
0<J<U
satisfy
\sinh 2J=e^{-2U}
. Then, there exist
n_{0}\geq 1
,
c>0
, and
\geq 1
, such that for any
n\geq n_{0}
, any
\ln(n)\leq K\leq\sqrt{n}
, and any
\eta_{L}\in\mathfrak{B}_{L},\eta_{R}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R}
with
\norm{\displace(\eta_L)}_{\infty}\leq K
,
\norm{\displace(\eta_R)}_{\infty}\leq K
,

\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}\big(\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(\Cr{cst:scaleForget}K^{2})\big)\geq 1-e^{-cK^{2}}.

Proof.

Recall (
63
). We will use this expression with
B=(\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(\Cr{cst:scaleForget}K^{2}))^{c}
by upper bounding the numerator and lower bounding the denominator. We start with the latter task:

\int d\mathrm{OZDec}(M;\gamma_{0}^{M+1})\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\gamma})=v_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{L}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{R}}\\
\geq\mathrm{C}e^{-c^{\prime}(|\eta_{L}|+|\eta_{R}|)}\mathrm{OZwalk}(T_{v_{n}-\mathrm{X}(\eta_{L})-\mathrm{X}(\eta_{R})}<\infty)\geq\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{n}}e^{-c^{\prime}K^{2}},

for some
C>0
,
c^{\prime}\geq 0
as in the proof of Lemma
8.9
. We then turn to the upper bound on the numerator. Note that under
(\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(\Cr{cst:scaleForget}K^{2}))^{c}
, one of the elements of
\gamma_{0}^{M+1}
must have a displacement with sup-norm at least
\Cr{cst:scaleForget}K^{2}-K
as
\norm{\displace(\eta_L)}_{\infty},\norm{\displace(\eta_R)}_{\infty}\leq K
. Moreover, as
\norm{v_n-(n,0)}_{\infty}\leq 2
, and
\mathrm{X}(\gamma)\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}\geq 1

p
-a.s.,
M\leq n+2
with full measure. Thus, as
p,p_{L},p_{R}
all have exponential tails,

\int d\mathrm{OZDec}(M;\gamma_{0}^{M+1})\mathds{1}_{(\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(\Cr{cst:scaleForget}K^{2}))^{c}}(\bar{\gamma})\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\gamma})=v_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{L}}\mathds{1}_{\bar{\gamma}\sim\eta_{R}}\\
\leq(n+4)e^{-c(\Cr{cst:scaleForget}K^{2}-K)}\leq e^{-c\Cr{cst:scaleForget}K^{2}}

as soon as
\Cr{cst:scaleForget}
is taken large enough. Thus, combining everything,

\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}\big((\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(\Cr{cst:scaleForget}K^{2}))^{c}\big)\leq\tfrac{\sqrt{n}}{C}e^{c^{\prime}K^{2}}e^{-c\Cr{cst:scaleForget}K^{2}}.

Taking
\Cr{cst:scaleForget}
large enough gives the wanted claim.
∎

We are now ready to define
\mathrm{MixMe}_{n}
. Rather than writing the explicit expression, we describe how to sample from it. Let
\max(\Cr{cst:scaleGCl},\Cr{cst:minMixScale})\ln(n)\leq\mathrm{sc}_{n}\leq\min(\epsilon_{0},1)\sqrt{n}
with
\epsilon_{0}
given by Lemma
8.9
, and assume
n
large enough and
m_{n},m_{n}^{\prime}\geq\Cr{cst:scaleMinBox}n
. For short, let
\mathrm{GCl}_{n}\equiv\mathrm{GCl}_{n}(\mathrm{sc}_{n})
.

•

First, sample
\mathcal{K}_{L},\mathcal{K}_{R}
under
\Phi_{n}(\cdot\,|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n})
;
recall Section
8.3
for their definition). They satisfy
\norm{\displace(\calK_L)}_{\infty}\leq 5\mathrm{sc}_{n}
a.s., and the same for
\mathcal{K}_{R}
, by construction.

•

Then, sample
\gamma_{0}^{M+1}
under
\mathrm{OZDec}_{\mathcal{K}_{L},\mathcal{K}_{R}}^{v_{n}}(\cdot\,|\,\mathrm{Forget}_{\mathcal{K}_{L},\mathcal{K}_{R}}^{v_{n}}(\Cr{cst:scaleForget}\mathrm{sc}_{n}^{2}))
and set
\mathcal{K}_{L}^{\prime}=\gamma_{0}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{\mathrm{T}_{\mathcal{K}_{L}}}
,
\mathcal{K}_{R}^{\prime}=\gamma_{\mathrm{T}_{\mathcal{K}_{R}}}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{M+1}
. They satisfy
\norm{\displace(\calK_L')}_{\infty}\leq\Cr{cst:scaleForget}\mathrm{sc}_{n}^{2}
by construction.

\mathrm{MixMe}_{n}
is then the law of
(\mathcal{K}_{L}^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{R}^{\prime})
.

Theorem
8.1
now follows from Lemmas
8.6
,
8.7
,
8.9
,
8.10
, and (
65
), and some routine considerations. Indeed, first note that by Lemmas
8.6
, and
8.4
,

\Phi_{n}(\mathcal{C}\notin\mathrm{GCl}_{n}\,|\,v_{n}\in\mathcal{C})=\frac{\Phi_{n}(\mathcal{C}\notin\mathrm{GCl}_{n},\,v_{n}\in\mathcal{C})}{\Phi_{n}(v_{n}\in\mathcal{C})}\leq C\sqrt{n}e^{-\nu_{1}n-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}e^{\nu_{1}n}\leq e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}

for
n
large enough.
So, the total variation distance between
\Phi_{n}(\cdot\,|\,v_{n}\in\mathcal{C})
and
\Phi_{n}(\cdot\,|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n})
is at most
e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}
, see Lemma
A.1
if more details are needed. Then, by Lemma
8.7
, see (
62
), the total variation distance between
\Phi_{n}(\mathcal{C}\in\cdot\,|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n})
and

\sum_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}\Phi_{n}\big(\mathcal{K}_{L}=\eta_{L},\,\mathcal{K}_{R}=\eta_{R}\,|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}\big)\Phi\big(\mathcal{C}\in\cdot\,|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n},\,\eta_{L}\sim\mathcal{C},\eta_{R}\sim\mathcal{C}\big)

is at most
e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}}
. Now, from Lemma
8.9
, we get that this last measure is at total variation distance at most
e^{-cn}
from the push-forward of

\sum_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}\Phi_{n}\big(\mathcal{K}_{L}=\eta_{L},\,\mathcal{K}_{R}=\eta_{R}\,|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}\big)\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}

by
\gamma_{0}^{M+1}\mapsto\gamma_{0}\circ\dots\circ\gamma_{M+1}
. Finally, by Lemma
8.10
, and direct considerations on total variation distance, see again Lemma
A.1
in case of need, this last measure is at total variation distance at most
e^{-c\mathrm{sc}_{n}^{2}}
from the pushforward of

\sum_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}\Phi_{n}\big(\mathcal{K}_{L}=\eta_{L},\,\mathcal{K}_{R}=\eta_{R}\,|\,\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{GCl}_{n}\big)\mathrm{OZDec}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}^{v_{n}}(\cdot\,|\,\mathrm{Forget}_{\eta_{L},\eta_{R}}(\Cr{cst:scaleForget}\mathrm{sc}_{n}^{2})).

Theorem
8.1
thus follows from this and a look at (
65
).

8.8.
Convergence to the Brownian Bridge

A general result of Kovchegov
[
Kov04
]
allows to deduce the invariance principle from Theorem
8.1
.
We start by introducing some notation.
Recall the constant
C_{0}
and the laws
p
,
\overline{\mathrm{MixMe}}_{n,m_{n}}
from Theorem
8.1
.
Let
m_{n}
be any sequence with
m_{n}\geq C_{0}n
,
p_{*}
be a probability measure on
\mathbb{Z}^{2}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}
defined as the push-forward of
p
by
\mathrm{X}
,
q_{*}^{n}
be a probability measure on
\big(\{-\lceil c_{0}\ln^{2}(n)\rceil,\dots,\lceil c_{0}\ln^{2}(n)\rceil\}^{2}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}\big)^{2}
defined as the push-forward of
\overline{\mathrm{MixMe}}_{n,m_{n}}
by
\mathrm{X}
.

Define all random variables on a common probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
. Let
X_{1},X_{2},\dots
be an i.i.d. sequence of random variables with law
p_{*}
. Let

\alpha=E(X_{1}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}),\quad\sigma^{2}_{x}=E((X_{1}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1})^{2})-\alpha^{2},\quad\sigma^{2}_{y}=E((Y_{1}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{2})^{2}),\quad\chi=\frac{\sigma^{2}_{y}}{\alpha}.

Since
\Phi
has a reflection symmetry, so do
p
and
p_{*}
).
Then, the mean vector and covariance matrix of
p_{*}
are given by

(66)

\bar{\mu}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\
0\end{pmatrix},\quad\mathrm{D}=\begin{pmatrix}\sigma^{2}_{x}&0\\
0&\sigma^{2}_{y}\end{pmatrix}.

Define

(67)

S_{0}:=(0,0),\quad S_{k}:=S_{k-1}+X_{k}.

Let
(V,W)\sim q^{n}_{*}
be independent of the
X_{i}
’s. Define

T_{n}:=\min\big\{k\geq 1:\ S_{k}=v_{R}-v_{L}-V-W\big\},

where
T_{n}:=\infty
when the set is empty.
When
T_{n}<\infty
, let
Z_{0}:=(0,0)
,
Z_{1}:=V
, and

Z_{k}:=\begin{cases}V+S_{k-1},&\text{ if }k=2,\dots T_{n}+1,\\
Z_{T_{n}+1}+W=v_{R}-v_{L}&\text{ if }k=T_{n}+2.\end{cases}

Further, for
s\in[0,2n+2]
, define

\tau_{-}(s):=\max\{k\geq 0:\ Z_{k}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}\leq s\},\quad\tau_{+}(s):=\min\{k\geq 0:\ Z_{k}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}\geq s\}.

and the linear interpolation:

\mathrm{LI}_{s}(Z)=\frac{Z_{\tau_{+}(s)}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}-s}{(Z_{\tau_{+}(s)}-Z_{\tau_{-}(s)})\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}}Z_{\tau_{-}(s)}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{2}+\frac{s-Z_{\tau_{-}(s)}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}}{(Z_{\tau_{+}(s)}-Z_{\tau_{-}(s)})\cdot\mathrm{e}_{1}}Z_{\tau_{+}(s)}\cdot\mathrm{e}_{2}

If
T_{n}=\infty
, set
\mathrm{LI}_{s}(Z):\equiv 0
.
Define also a re-scaled version of
(\mathrm{LI}_{s}(Z))_{s\in[0,2n+2]}
:

\Gamma_{t}^{n}=\Gamma_{t}^{n}(Z)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2n\chi}}\mathrm{LI}_{t(2n+2)}(Z),\quad t\in[0,1].

The next Lemma concludes our study of the invariance principle for the (modified) ATRC cluster.

Lemma 8.11
.

The sequence of random functions
(\Gamma_{t}^{n})_{t\in[0,1]}
under
\mathbb{P}
conditioned on
T_{n}<\infty
, converges weakly to a standard Brownian Bridge. Moreover, with probability going to
1
as
n\to\infty
, the maximal step of
Z
has norm less than
C\ln^{2}(n)
for some
C\geq 0
.

Proof.

[
Kov04
, Technical Theorem]
proves a general result about convergence of directed random walk bridges in dimension
d
to the graph of a Brownian bridge in dimension
d-1
.
In our setup, this result gives the following.
Consider a directed random walk with iid steps having exponential tails and mean equal to
a\mathrm{e}_{1}
with
a>0
.
Now, look at a sequence of bridges for this walk from
0
to
x_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
Then, for any
\varepsilon>0
, the sequence of the bridges converges, under linear scaling in coordinate
\mathrm{e}_{1}
and diffusive scaling in coordinate
\mathrm{e}_{2}
, to the graph of a
1D
Brownian bridge uniformly in
\norm{x_n - (n,0)}\leq n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
.

Our case satisfies these conditions: Theorem
8.1
gives the wanted properties for the steps;
the walk starts at
V
and ends at
W
, where
\norm{V}
and
\norm{W-(2n,0)}
are a.s. bounded by
c\ln^{2}n
.
Thus, we get the convergence.
The estimate on the maximal step size is a simple large deviation estimate combined with the local limit theorem.
∎

Remark 8.12
.

Note that the value of the diffusivity constant
\chi
can be identified with the curvature of
\partial\mathcal{W}
at
\nu(\mathrm{e}_{1})\mathrm{e}_{1}
, exactly as in
[
IOSV22
, Appendix B]
.

9.
Proofs of Theorems
1.1
,
1.3
,
1.5
and
3.6

Previous sections establish coupling between the FK-percolation and the (modified) ATRC models and several results for the latter: mixing properties, Ornstein–Zernike theory, invariance principle for the interface under suitable Dobrushin boundary conditions.
In order to transfer the invariance principle to the FK-percolation, we need to show proximity of interfaces in the two models.

Consider the coupling measure
\Psi^{1/0}
constructed in Section
4
.
A crucial feature of this coupling is that the respective interfaces stay close to each other with high probability. To measure the distance between interfaces, we will work with the
one-sided Hausdorff distance
defined by

\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(R,S):=\sup_{x\in R}\inf_{y\in S}\mathrm{d}_{\infty}(x,y),\qquad R,S\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}.

Given a bi-infinite connected set
C\subset\mathbb{L}_{\bullet}\cap(\mathbb{R}\times[-c,c])
, we say that a subset
R\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is (weakly)
above

C
if it is contained in (the closure of) the connected component of the point
(0,c+1)
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus C
, where we identify
C
with the union of line segments between the endpoints of the edges in
\mathbb{E}_{C}
. We say that
R
is (weakly)
below

C
if it is contained in (the closure of) the connected component of the point
(0,-c-1)
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus C
. We make the analogous definitions for finite connected sets by extending them to bi-finite connected sets by attaching left-infinite horizontal lines to all the leftmost points of the finite set and right-infinite horizontal lines to all the rightmost points; and, analogously, for connected subsets
C\subset\mathbb{L}_{\circ}
.
Recall the upper and lower envelopes
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{\pm,n}
in
G_{n}
defined in Section
1
.
Define
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{\pm,n,m}
in
G=G_{n,m}
analogously.
Recall also the definition of
\Gamma_{\mathtt{ATRC}}^{n,m}
in Section
4.4
.

Lemma 9.1
.

There exist constants
C,c>0
such that, for any
n,m,k\geq 1
,

\Psi^{1/0}(\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{\pm,n,m},\Gamma_{\mathtt{ATRC}}^{n,m})>k)\leq Cnme^{-ck}.

Proof.

Let us fix
n,m\geq 1
and omit them from the notation.
We present the argument for
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{+}
. The statement for
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{-}
is proved in an analogous fashion.
Consider the coupling
\Psi^{1/0}
of
\omega\sim\mathtt{FK}_{G}^{1/0},\ (\sigma_{\bullet},\sigma_{\circ})\sim\mathtt{Spin}_{\mathcal{D}}^{+,+-}
and
(\omega_{\tau},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}})\sim\mathtt{mATRC}_{K}
described in Section
4.2
.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{v_{L}^{\prime}}
be the cluster of
v^{\prime}_{L}:=(-n-\tfrac{1}{2},-\tfrac{1}{2})
in
\omega^{*}
; see Fig.
29
.

Figure 29.
For
n=m=3
. Left: vertices of
G
(solid) and its dual (hollow) with “
1/0
” boundary conditions (black solid and dashed edges), the dual cluster
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=C^{\prime}
(endpoints of dashed edges), and the lowermost path
p_{C^{\prime}}^{\shortuparrow}
(blue solid edges). Center: the set
\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}
(solid and hollow vertices), the tiles in
A_{C^{\prime}}
that intersect them, with boundary tiles in
A_{C^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{b}}
in grey. Right: The associated edges in
\bar{E}^{C^{\prime}}
, with those in
E_{\mathrm{b}}^{C^{\prime}}
contained in grey tiles.

Note that

(68)

\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{+},\Gamma_{\mathtt{ATRC}})\leq\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{+},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})+\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\Gamma_{\mathtt{ATRC}})\leq\tfrac{1}{2}+\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\Gamma_{\mathtt{ATRC}}).

Let
p_{-}\subset\mathbb{V}_{*E}
be the uppermost path connecting
v^{\prime}_{L}
to
v^{\prime}_{R}:=(n+\tfrac{1}{2},-\tfrac{1}{2})
on which
\sigma_{\circ}\equiv-1
.
Recall the definition of
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{v_{L}}
in Section
4.4
.
Since
*\partial^{\mathrm{edge}}\mathcal{C}
contains such a path, as well as a path connecting
(-n-\tfrac{3}{2},\tfrac{1}{2})
to
(n+\tfrac{3}{2},\tfrac{1}{2})
on which
\sigma_{\circ}\equiv 1
(see Fig.
9
and
10
), we have

(69)

\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\Gamma_{\mathtt{ATRC}})\leq\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},p_{-})+\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(p_{-},\Gamma_{\mathtt{ATRC}})\leq\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},p_{-})+\tfrac{1}{2}.

Fix a realisation
C^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
By the coupling,
\sigma_{\circ}\equiv-1
on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=C^{\prime}
(see Fig.
9
). Thus, the path
p_{-}
cannot contain vertices below
C^{\prime}
.
Hence, the random variable
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(C^{\prime},p_{-})
is measurable with respect to
\sigma_{\circ}
restricted to the vertices in
\mathcal{D}\cap\mathbb{L}_{\circ}
above
C^{\prime}
.
Let us determine the conditional law of
(\sigma_{\bullet},\sigma_{\circ})
restricted to the vertices in
\mathcal{D}
above
C^{\prime}
.

Let
p_{C^{\prime}}^{\shortuparrow}
be the lowermost path in
\omega
connecting
v_{L}
to
v_{R}
above
C^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}
be the subset of vertices in
\mathcal{D}
above
p_{C^{\prime}}^{\shortuparrow}
.
Denote by
A_{C^{\prime}}
the set of tiles with at least one corner in
\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}
, and by
A_{C^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{b}}
the set of tiles with precisely one corner in
\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}
; see Fig.
29
.
Define the associated sets of edges

\bar{E}^{C^{\prime}}=\{e_{t}:t\in A_{C^{\prime}}\},\quad E_{\mathrm{b}}^{C^{\prime}}=\{e_{t}:t\in A_{C^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{b}}\}\quad\text{and}\quad E^{C^{\prime}}=\bar{E}^{C^{\prime}}\setminus E_{\mathrm{b}}^{C^{\prime}}.

Set
G_{C^{\prime}}=(\mathbb{V}_{E^{C^{\prime}}},E^{C^{\prime}})
,
K_{C^{\prime}}=(\mathbb{V}_{\bar{E}^{C^{\prime}}},\bar{E}^{C^{\prime}})
, and let
K_{C^{\prime}}^{1}
be the graph obtained from
K_{C^{\prime}}
by identifying vertices in
\partial^{\mathrm{in}}_{\mathbb{L}_{\bullet}}\mathbb{V}_{\bar{E}^{C^{\prime}}}
and those connected by
p_{C^{\prime}}^{\shortuparrow}
.

By Lemma
4.2
and the domain Markov property of FK-percolation,

\Psi^{1/0}(\omega|_{E^{C^{\prime}}}=\omega_{0}\,|\,\mathcal{C}^{\prime}={C^{\prime}})=\mathtt{FK}_{G}^{1/0}(\omega|_{E^{C^{\prime}}}=\omega_{0}\,|\,\mathcal{C}^{\prime}={C^{\prime}})=\mathtt{FK}_{G_{C^{\prime}}}^{1}(\omega|_{E^{C^{\prime}}}=\omega_{0})

for any
\omega_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{E^{C^{\prime}}}
.
The FK-percolation measure with wired boundary conditions can be coupled to the six-vertex spin measure with
(+,+)
boundary conditions.
This version of the BKW coupling was presented in
[
GP23
,
Lis21
]
and is very closely related to our Lemma
4.2
that deals with the Dobrushin boundary conditions.
We provide only the statement.

We first define the six-vertex spin measure on
\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}
under
(+,+)
boundary conditions (compare to (
11
)):
for
\sigma=(\sigma_{\bullet},\sigma_{\circ})\in\{\pm 1\}^{\mathbb{L}_{\bullet}}\times\{\pm 1\}^{\mathbb{L}_{\circ}}
, it is defined by

\mathtt{Spin}_{\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}}^{+,+}(\sigma)\propto\mathbf{c}^{|T_{5,6}^{\mathrm{i}}(\sigma)|}\,\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{b}}^{|T_{5,6}^{\mathrm{b}}(\sigma)|}\,\mathds{1}_{\sigma=+1\text{ on }(\mathbb{L}_{\bullet}\cup\mathbb{L}_{\circ})\setminus\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{ice}}(\sigma),

where
T_{5,6}^{\mathrm{i}}(\sigma)
and
T_{5,6}^{\mathrm{b}}(\sigma)
are respectively the sets of tiles in
A_{C^{\prime}}\setminus A_{C^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{b}}
and
A_{C^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{b}}
that are of types 5,6 in
\sigma
.
Then, as in Lemma
4.2
,

\Psi^{1/0}\big((\sigma_{\bullet}|_{\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}\cap\mathbb{L}_{\bullet}},\sigma_{\circ}|_{\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}\cap\mathbb{L}_{\circ}})\in\cdot\,\big|\,\mathcal{C}^{\prime}={C^{\prime}}\big)=\mathtt{Spin}_{\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}}^{+,+}|_{\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}}.

In the same vein, we determine the conditional law of
(\omega_{\tau},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}})
on
\bar{E}^{C^{\prime}}
by adapting Lemma
4.4
to
+,+
boundary conditions.
We now define a modified ATRC measure on
K_{C^{\prime}}
by taking Definition
4.3
and replacing
(E,E_{\mathrm{b}}^{+},E_{\mathrm{b}}^{-})
with
(E^{C^{\prime}},E_{\mathrm{b}}^{C^{\prime}},\varnothing)
(in particular,
I(\mathcal{C})\equiv 0
for all clusters): for any
(a,b)\in\{0,1\}^{\bar{E}^{C^{\prime}}}\times\{0,1\}^{\bar{E}^{C^{\prime}}}
, define

\mathtt{mATRC}_{K_{C^{\prime}}}^{\varnothing}(a,b)\propto\mathds{1}_{a\subseteq b}\mathds{1}_{b\setminus a\subseteq E^{C^{\prime}}}2^{\kappa_{K_{C^{\prime}}}(a)+\kappa_{K_{C^{\prime}}^{1}}(b)}2^{|a|}(\mathbf{c}-2)^{|b\setminus a|}(\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{b}}-1)^{|E_{\mathrm{b}}^{C^{\prime}}\setminus b|}.

Since
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}={C^{\prime}}
, we have
\sigma_{\bullet}\equiv 1
at all endpoints of edges in
E_{\mathrm{b}}^{C^{\prime}}
.
Then, as in Lemma
4.4
,

\Psi^{1/0}\big((\omega_{\tau},\omega_{\tau\tau^{\prime}})|_{\bar{E}^{C^{\prime}}}\in\cdot\,\big|\,\mathcal{C}^{\prime}={C^{\prime}}\big)=\mathtt{mATRC}_{K_{C^{\prime}}}^{\varnothing}.

We claim that this measure satisfies (
strong-FKG
).
Indeed, all local terms in the right side of the display trivially satisfy the property and the non-local terms assign weight two to the clusters in
a
and
b
.
It is standard that such terms satisfy (
strong-FKG
): see the classical
[
Gri06
, Theorem 3.8]
for the case of the random-cluster model; alternatively, the proof of Lemma
4.6
applies by setting
\alpha\equiv\beta\equiv\tfrac{1}{2}
.
Thus,

\mathtt{mATRC}_{K_{C^{\prime}}}^{\varnothing}\leq_{\mathrm{st}}\mathtt{mATRC}_{K_{C^{\prime}}}^{\varnothing}(\cdot\,|\,\omega_{\tau}(e)=1\text{ for all }e\in E_{\mathrm{b}}^{C^{\prime}})=\mathtt{ATRC}_{G_{C^{\prime}}}^{1,1}.

As in the proof of Lemma
8.2
, uniform exponential decay of connection probabilities in
\mathtt{ATRC}_{G_{C^{\prime}}}^{1,1}
(Theorem
3.5
) and the arguments of
[
Ale04
, Section 2]
imply that connection probabilities in
\mathtt{mATRC}_{K_{C^{\prime}}}^{\varnothing}
decay exponentially as well.

Finally, since
\sigma_{\circ}\sim\omega_{\tau}^{*}
and
\sigma_{\circ}=1
on
\mathbb{L}_{\circ}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{C^{\prime}}
, any connected component of
\{\sigma_{\circ}=-1\}
must be surrounded by a circuit in
\omega_{\tau}
, whence

\Psi^{1/0}(\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},p_{-})>k\,|\,\mathcal{C}^{\prime}={C^{\prime}})\leq\mathtt{mATRC}_{K_{C^{\prime}}}^{\varnothing}(\exists\,i\in p_{C^{\prime}}^{\shortuparrow}:\,i\xleftrightarrow{\omega_{\tau}}i+\partial^{\mathrm{in}}\Lambda_{k})\leq Cnmke^{-ck}.

Averaging over
C^{\prime}
and inserting this in (
68
) and (
69
) completes the proof.
∎

9.1.
Proof of Theorem
1.3

We will derive Theorem
1.3
from the analogous statement for the cluster
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{v_{L}}
of
v_{L}
in
\omega_{\tau}
with law the first marginal of
\mathtt{mATRC}_{K_{n,m}}(\cdot\,|\,v_{L}\xleftrightarrow{\omega_{\tau}}v_{R})
(Theorem
8.1
and Lemma
8.11
) and the proximity statement (Lemma
9.1
).

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

To lighten the notation, we omit
p_{c}(q),q
from the subscripts.
Recall the definition of the upper and lower envelopes
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{\pm,n}
in
G_{n}
defined in Section
1
, and of
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{\pm,n,m}
in
G=G_{n,m}
defined analogously.
We will first show the statement for the FK measure
\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n,Cn}}^{1/0}
for
C
sufficiently large, and then from it derive the statement for
\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n}}^{1/0}
.

By Theorem
8.1
and Lemmata
8.11
and
9.1
, there exists
C>1
such that the statement holds for
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{\pm,n,Cn}
under the measure
\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n,Cn}}^{1/0}
.
The derivation for
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{\pm,n}
under
\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n}}^{1/0}
follows from the FKG and DLR properties of FK percolation, and we only sketch the argument.
Define the graphs
G_{n,Cn}^{\pm}=(V_{n,Cn}^{\pm},E_{n,Cn}^{\pm})
by

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n,Cn}^{-}:=\{-n,\dots,n\}\times\{-Cn,\dots,n\},\quad\Lambda_{n,Cn}^{+}:=\{-n,\dots,n\}\times\{-n,\dots,Cn\},

\displaystyle V_{n,Cn}^{\pm}=\Lambda_{n,Cn}^{\pm}\cup(\partial^{\mathrm{ex}}\Lambda_{n,Cn}^{\pm}\cap\mathbb{H}^{+}),\quad E_{n,Cn}^{\pm}=\mathbb{E}_{V_{n,Cn}^{\pm}}\setminus\mathbb{E}_{(\Lambda_{n,Cn}^{\pm})^{c}}.

Then, by the FKG and DLR properties of the FK measures, it holds that

\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n,Cn}}^{1/0}\geq_{\mathrm{st}}\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n,Cn}^{+}}^{1/0}\leq_{\mathrm{st}}\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n}}^{1/0}\leq_{\mathrm{st}}\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n,Cn}^{-}}^{1/0}\geq_{\mathrm{st}}\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n,Cn}}^{1/0}.

If
\omega
is distributed according to
\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n,Cn}}^{1/0}
, by the statement for
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{\pm,n,Cn}
under this measure, there exists a left-right crossing above
[-n,n]\times\{0\}
in
\omega
and one in
\omega^{*}
below it at arbitrary small linear distance from
[-n,n]\times\{0\}
.
A chain of classical monotone coupling arguments finishes the proof.
∎

9.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

We will derive Theorem
1.1
from Theorem
1.3
.
Recall the definition of the one-sided Hausdorff distance
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}
, and the notion of a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
being above or below a connected set in
\mathbb{L}_{\bullet}
or
\mathbb{L}_{\circ}
, introduced above Lemma
9.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

To simplify the notation, we omit
n,q,T_{c}(q),p_{c}(q)
from sub and superscripts. Consider the Edwards–Sokal coupling
\mathtt{ES}_{\Lambda}^{1/0}
of
\sigma\sim\mathtt{Potts}_{\Lambda}^{1/\mathrm{f}}
and
\omega\sim\mathtt{FK}_{G_{n}}^{1/0}
described in the introduction; see
[
Gri06
, Section 1.4]
for details.
Observe that the FK and Potts envelopes deterministically satisfy

\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{-}(k)\leq\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{+}(k),\quad\Gamma_{\mathtt{Potts}}^{-}(k)\leq\Gamma_{\mathtt{Potts}}^{+}(k),\quad\Gamma_{\mathtt{Potts}}^{\pm}(k)\leq\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{\pm}(k)\quad\forall k\in\{-n,\dots,n\}.

In particular,
\Gamma_{\mathtt{Potts}}^{+}
is “sandwiched” between
\Gamma_{\mathtt{Potts}}^{-}
and
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{+}
.
By Theorem
1.3
, it suffices to show the existence of
c,C>0
for which, for any
k\geq 1
,

\mathtt{ES}_{\Lambda}^{1/0}(\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\Gamma_{\mathtt{Potts}}^{-},\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{-})>k)<Cn^{2}e^{-ck}.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be the cluster of the lower boundary in
\{\sigma\neq 1\}
, that is, the set of
i\in\Lambda
for which there exists a path
(i_{0},\dots,i_{\ell})
in
\Lambda
with
i_{0}=i,\,i_{\ell}\in\partial^{\mathrm{in}}\Lambda\cap\mathbb{H}^{-}
and
\sigma(i_{k})\neq 1
for
0\leq k\leq\ell
.
By definition, each point in the lower envelope
\Gamma_{\mathtt{Potts}}^{-}
is weakly above
\mathcal{C}
.
Therefore, it suffices to show that
\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{-}
is not far above
\mathcal{C}
.
Fix a realisation
C
of
\mathcal{C}
, and define
\Lambda_{C}=\Lambda\setminus(C\cup\partial^{\mathrm{ex}}C)
. By the coupling and the spatial Markov property of the Potts model, it holds that

\mathtt{ES}_{\Lambda}^{1/0}(\sigma|_{\Lambda_{C}}\in\cdot\,|\,\mathcal{C}=C)=\mathtt{Potts}_{\Lambda}^{1/\mathrm{f}}(\sigma|_{\Lambda_{C}}\in\cdot\,|\,\mathcal{C}=C)=\mathtt{Potts}_{\Lambda_{C}}^{1}.

Let
G_{C}=(V_{C},E_{C})
be defined by
E_{C}=\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{C}}\cup\partial^{\mathrm{edge}}\Lambda_{C}
and
V_{C}=\mathbb{V}_{E_{C}}
. Then, by the above and by the coupling,

\mathtt{ES}_{\Lambda}^{1/0}(\omega|_{E_{C}}\in\cdot\,|\,\mathcal{C}=C)=\mathtt{FK}_{G_{C}}^{1}(\omega|_{E_{C}}\in\cdot).

Now, conditional on
\mathcal{C}=C
, if
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(C,\Gamma_{\mathtt{FK}}^{-})>k
, then there exists a dual path of length
k
in
\omega^{*}|_{*E_{C}}
. Since
\mathtt{FK}_{G_{C}}^{1}
stochastically dominates the infinite-volume measure
\mathtt{FK}^{1}
, which admits exponential decay of connection probabilites in its dual
[
DGH+21
, Theorem 1.2]
, the proof is complete.
∎

9.3.
Proof of Theorems
1.5
and
3.6

Finally, the Ornstein-Zernike asymptotics for the two-point function of the AT and ATRC models (Theorems
1.5
and
3.6
) are direct consequences of Theorem
7.1
.

Proof of Theorems
1.5
and
3.6
.

Theorem
7.1
gives, in particular, that for
s\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
, one can find
t\in\partial\mathcal{W},\delta\in(0,1)
such that (sums are over pieces confined in the cones/diamonds obtained using
\mathcal{Y}^{\blacktriangleleft}_{t,\delta}
)

\Big|\mathrm{C}\sum_{\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}}p_{L}(\gamma_{L})p_{R}(\gamma_{R})\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{k}}\mathds{1}_{\mathrm{X}(\bar{\gamma})=ns}\prod_{i=1}^{k}p(\gamma_{k})\\
-e^{n\nu(s)}\mathtt{ATRC}_{J,U}(0\xleftrightarrow{\omega_{\tau}}ns)\Big|\leq C_{1}e^{-c_{1}n},

which is a comparison between
e^{n\nu(s)}\mathtt{ATRC}_{J,U}(0\leftrightarrow ns)
and the Green functions of a directed random walk on
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
(the push-forward of
p_{L},p_{R},p
by
\mathrm{X}
). One can then use the local limit theorem in dimension 2 as in
[
AOV24
, Section 8.1]
to obtain

(70)

e^{n\nu(s)}\mathtt{ATRC}_{J,U}(0\leftrightarrow ns)=\frac{c(s)}{\sqrt{n}}(1+o_{n}(1)),

which is the wanted asymptotics for the ATRC model. The claim for the AT model follows from the coupling (
8
) between
\mathtt{AT}
and
\mathtt{ATRC}
, .
∎

Appendix A
Total variation distance

Let
\Omega
be countable or finite. Consider the measure space
\Omega\equiv(\Omega,\mathcal{P}(\Omega))
. Recall that for two finites measures
\mu,\nu
on
\Omega
, one can define their total variation distance by

\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu,\nu)\coloneq\sup_{A\subset\Omega}|\mu(A)-\nu(A)|=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}|\mu(\omega)-\nu(\omega)|=\sup_{\norm{f}_{\infty}\leq 1}\bigl|\mu[f]-\nu[f]\bigr|

where the second supremum is over functions
f:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
, and we denoted
\mu[f]=\int fd\mu
. In the case of
\mu,\nu
probability measures,
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu,\nu)=\min_{\pi\in\Pi(\mu,\nu)}\pi(X\neq Y)
where
\Pi(\mu,\nu)
is the set of couplings of
\mu
and
\nu
, and
(X,Y)\sim\pi
.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\Omega
be countable or finite. Let
\mu,\nu
be two finites measures on
(\Omega,\mathcal{P}(\Omega))
. Suppose
\mu(\Omega)=1
. Let
\epsilon\in[0,1)
,
A\subset\Omega
. Then, one has the following.

(1)

If
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu,\nu)\leq\epsilon
,
\nu(\Omega)\in[1-\epsilon,1+\epsilon]
, and

\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\nu,\bar{\nu})\leq\tfrac{\epsilon}{2},\quad\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu,\bar{\nu})\leq\tfrac{3\epsilon}{2}.

where
\bar{\nu}(B)=\frac{\nu(B)}{\nu(\Omega)}
is the normalized version of
\nu
.

(2)

If
\mu(A)\geq 1-\epsilon
, then,

\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu,\mu_{A})\leq\tfrac{3\epsilon}{4},

where
\mu_{A}(B)=\frac{\mu(A\cap B)}{\mu(A)}
is the conditional measure.

Proof.

Start with the first point. By the assumption,
|\nu(\Omega)-1|=|\nu(\Omega)-\mu(\Omega)|\leq\epsilon
. Then,
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\nu,\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\omega}\nu(\omega)|1-\frac{1}{\nu(\Omega)}|=\frac{1}{2}|\nu(\Omega)-1|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}
. The second part of the display follows by triangle inequality.

The second point follows form the first using the measure
\nu(B)=\mu(A\cap B)
, which satisfies
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu,\nu)=\frac{\nu(\Omega\setminus A)}{2}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}
.
∎

Appendix B
Mixing to ratio mixing

We prove here a technical Lemma whose use is recurrent in the proof that mixing implies ratio mixing under suitable conditions. It is a simplified version of the argument in
[
Ale98
, Section 5]
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\Omega_{i},\,i=1,2
be finite sets. Let
\Omega=\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2}
and
\mathcal{F}_{i}=\{A\subset\Omega_{i}\}
,
\mathcal{F}=\{A\subset\Omega\}
. Let
\mu,\nu
be positive probability measures on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F})
. Let

\pi_{i}:\Omega\to\Omega_{i},\quad\pi_{i}((\omega_{1},\omega_{2}))=\omega_{i},\quad\mu_{i}=\mu\circ\pi_{i}^{-1}.

Let
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{3}\in[0,1)
. Suppose that all of the following hold:

(1)

Mixing of
\mu,\nu
: for every
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in\Omega_{1}
,
A\subset\Omega_{2}
,
\rho\in\{\mu,\nu\}

|\rho(\Omega_{1}\times A\,|\,\{\xi\}\times\Omega_{2})-\rho(\Omega_{1}\times A\,|\,\{\xi^{\prime}\}\times\Omega_{2})|\leq\epsilon_{1}

(2)

Proximity between second marginals:
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu_{2},\nu_{2})\leq\epsilon_{2}
;

(3)

Conditional equality: there exists an event
D\subset\Omega_{2}
such that for
\rho\in\{\mu_{2},\nu_{2}\}
,
\rho(D)\geq 1-\epsilon_{3}
, and for any
y\in D
,

\frac{\mu(x,y)}{\mu_{2}(y)}=\frac{\nu(x,y)}{\nu_{2}(y)},\ \forall x\in\Omega_{1}.

Then, if
\epsilon=\max(\epsilon_{1},\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}},\epsilon_{3})\leq 0.1
, then, for any
x\in\Omega_{1}
,

1-9\epsilon\leq\frac{\mu_{1}(x)}{\nu_{1}(x)}\leq\frac{1}{1-9\epsilon}.

Proof.

The proof goes by constructing a suitable coupling of
\mu,\nu
. Let
Q
be a maximal coupling of
\mu_{2}
and
\nu_{2}
. Then, sample a random vector
(X,Y)=\big((X_{1},X_{2}),(Y_{1},Y_{2})\big)
as follows:

(1)

sample
(X_{2},Y_{2})
using
Q
;

(2)

sample
X_{1}
using
\mu(\ \,|\,\Omega_{1}\times\{X_{2}\})\circ\pi_{1}^{-1}
;

(3)

if
X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D
, set
Y_{1}=X_{1}
. Else, sample
Y_{1}\sim\nu(\ \,|\,\Omega_{1}\times\{Y_{2}\})\circ\pi_{1}^{-1}
independently of
X_{1}
.

Denote
\Psi
the law of
(X,Y)
.

Claim B.2
.

All the following points hold.

(1)

\Psi
is a coupling of
\mu
and
\nu
.

(2)

(X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D)\implies(X_{1}=Y_{1})
.

(3)

X_{1}
and
Y_{2}
are independent conditionally on
X_{2}
, and
Y_{1}
and
X_{2}
are independent conditionally on
Y_{2}
.

Proof.

The second point is by construction. We check the first point. For
x=(x_{1},x_{2})\in\Omega
,

\displaystyle\Psi(X=x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y_{2}\in\Omega_{2}}Q(x_{2},y_{2})\Psi(X_{1}=x_{1}\,|\,X_{2}=x_{2},Y_{2}=y_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{y_{2}\in\Omega_{2}}Q(x_{2},y_{2})\frac{\mu(x_{1},x_{2})}{\mu_{2}(x_{2})}=\mu(x_{1},x_{2}).

Also, for
y=(y_{1},y_{2})\in\Omega
,

\displaystyle\Psi(Y=y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{2}\in\Omega_{2}}Q(x_{2},y_{2})\Psi(Y_{1}=y_{1}\,|\,X_{2}=x_{2},Y_{2}=y_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{2}\in\Omega_{2}}Q(x_{2},y_{2})\Big(\mathds{1}_{y_{2}=x_{2}\in D}\frac{\mu(y_{1},x_{2})}{\mu_{2}(x_{2})}+(1-\mathds{1}_{y_{2}=x_{2}\in D})\frac{\nu(y_{1},y_{2})}{\nu_{2}(y_{2})}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{2}\in\Omega_{2}}Q(x_{2},y_{2})\frac{\nu(y_{1},y_{2})}{\nu_{2}(y_{2})}=\nu(y_{1},y_{2}),

as, by hypotheses on
D
,
\mathds{1}_{y_{2}=x_{2}\in D}\frac{\mu(y_{1},x_{2})}{\mu_{2}(x_{2})}=\mathds{1}_{y_{2}=x_{2}\in D}\frac{\mu(y_{1},y_{2})}{\mu_{2}(y_{2})}=\mathds{1}_{y_{2}=x_{2}\in D}\frac{\nu(y_{1},y_{2})}{\nu_{2}(y_{2})}
.

Let us finally check the third point. First, for any
x_{1}\in\Omega_{1},x_{2},y_{2}\in\Omega_{2}
,

\Psi(X_{1}=x_{1}\,|\,X_{2}=x_{2},Y_{2}=y_{2})=\frac{\mu(x_{1},x_{2})}{\mu_{2}(x_{2})}=\Psi(X_{1}=x_{1}\,|\,X_{2}=x_{2}).

Then, for any
y_{1}\in\Omega_{1},x_{2},y_{2}\in\Omega_{2}
,

\Psi(Y_{1}=y_{1}\,|\,X_{2}=x_{2},Y_{2}=y_{2})=\mathds{1}_{y_{2}=x_{2}\in D}\frac{\mu(y_{1},x_{2})}{\mu_{2}(x_{2})}+(1-\mathds{1}_{y_{2}=x_{2}\in D})\frac{\nu(y_{1},y_{2})}{\nu_{2}(y_{2})}\\
=\frac{\nu(y_{1},y_{2})}{\nu_{2}(y_{2})}=\Psi(Y_{1}=y_{1}\,|\,Y_{2}=y_{2}),

by hypotheses on
D
, as before.
∎

Introduce then
g,h:\Omega_{2}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
defined by

\displaystyle g(\xi)=\Psi(X_{2}\neq Y_{2}\,|\,X_{2}=\xi),

\displaystyle h(\xi)=\Psi(X_{2}\neq Y_{2}\,|\,Y_{2}=\xi).

Define the event
H
by

H=\{X_{2}=Y_{2}\}\cap\{X_{2}\in D\}\cap\{Y_{2}\in D\}\cap\{g(X_{2})\leq\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}}\}\cap\{h(Y_{2})\leq\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}}\}.

Claim B.3
.

One has

\displaystyle\max_{x_{1}\in\Omega_{2}}\Psi(H^{c}\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})\leq 9\epsilon,

\displaystyle\max_{y_{1}\in\Omega_{2}}\Psi(H^{c}\,|\,Y_{1}=y_{1})\leq 9\epsilon.

Proof.

Let
a=\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}}
. First, let
\{g>a\}=\{x_{2}\in\Omega_{2}:\ g(x_{2})>a\}
, one has

\mu(\Omega_{1}\times\{g>a\})=\Psi(g(X_{2})>a)\leq a^{-1}\Psi(g(X_{2}))=a^{-1}\Psi(\Psi(X_{2}\neq Y_{2}\,|\,X_{2}))\\
=a^{-1}\Psi(X_{2}\neq Y_{2})\leq\frac{\epsilon_{2}}{a},

as
\Psi(X_{2}\neq Y_{2})=Q(X_{2}\neq Y_{2})=\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu_{2},\nu_{2})\leq\epsilon_{2}
, and the same for
\nu(\Omega_{1}\times\{h>a\})
. Now, by our first hypotheses,

\Psi(g(X_{2})>a\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})=\mu(\Omega_{1}\times\{g>a\}\,|\,\{x_{1}\}\times\Omega_{2})\\
\leq\mu(\Omega_{1}\times\{g>a\})+\epsilon_{1}=\frac{\epsilon_{2}}{a}+\epsilon_{1},

and the same for
\Psi(h(Y_{2})>a\,|\,Y_{1}=y_{1})
. Also,

\Psi(X_{2}\in D^{c}\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})=\mu(\Omega_{1}\times D^{c}\,|\,\{x_{1}\}\times\Omega_{2})\leq\mu(\Omega_{1}\times D^{c})+\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{3}+\epsilon_{1},

and the same for
\Psi(Y_{2}\in D^{c}\,|\,Y_{1}=y_{1})
.

Then, as
X_{1}
and
Y_{2}
are independent conditionally on
X_{2}
(by Claim
B.2
),

\displaystyle\Psi(

\displaystyle X_{2}\neq Y_{2},g(X_{2})\leq a\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{x_{2}:g(x_{2})\leq a}\Psi(X_{2}=x_{2}\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})\Psi(Y_{2}\neq x_{2}\,|\,X_{2}=x_{2})

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{x_{2}:g(x_{2})\leq a}\Psi(X_{2}=x_{2}\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})g(x_{2})\leq a.

In the same fashion,
\Psi(X_{2}\neq Y_{2},h(Y_{2})\leq a\,|\,Y_{1}=y_{1})\leq a
. Finally,

\displaystyle\Psi(

\displaystyle X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D,h(Y_{2})>a\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad=\Psi(X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})\Psi(h(Y_{2})>a\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1},X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D)

\displaystyle\qquad=\Psi(X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})\Psi(h(Y_{2})>a\,|\,Y_{1}=x_{1},X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\Psi(X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})}{\Psi(X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D\,|\,Y_{1}=x_{1})}\Psi(X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D,h(Y_{2})>a\,|\,Y_{1}=x_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\epsilon_{2}/a+\epsilon_{1}}{1-2\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{2}/a-a},

where the second equality is because
X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D\implies X_{1}=Y_{1}
, and the inequality is the previous bounds, and a union bound on
\Psi(\{X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D\}^{c}\,|\,Y_{1}=x_{1})
. Putting things together,

\displaystyle\Psi(H^{c}\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\Psi(X_{2}\in D^{c}\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})+\Psi(g(X_{2})>a\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\Psi(X_{2}\neq Y_{2},g(X_{2})\leq a\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\Psi(X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D,h(Y_{2})>a\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{3}+\epsilon_{1}+\frac{\epsilon_{2}}{a}+\epsilon_{1}+a+\frac{\epsilon_{2}/a+\epsilon_{1}}{1-2\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{2}/a-a}.

One obtains the same bound on
\Psi(H^{c}\,|\,Y_{1}=y_{1})
in the same way (with
h\leftrightarrow g
and
Y_{2}\leftrightarrow X_{2}
). Plugging in
a=\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}}
and using the definition of
\epsilon
gives that the last display is upper bounded by

5\epsilon+\frac{2\epsilon}{1-5\epsilon}\leq 9\epsilon,

as
\epsilon\leq 0.1
by hypotheses.
∎

Let us now see how Claims
B.2
and
B.3
imply the wanted result. As
\Psi
is a coupling,

\frac{\mu_{1}(\xi)}{\nu_{1}(\xi)}=\frac{\Psi(X_{1}=\xi)}{\Psi(Y_{1}=\xi)}=\frac{\Psi(X_{1}=\xi)\Psi(Y_{1}=\xi,H)}{\Psi(Y_{1}=\xi)\Psi(X_{1}=\xi,H)}=\frac{\Psi(H\,|\,Y_{1}=\xi)}{\Psi(H\,|\,X_{1}=\xi)},

where the second equality is because
\{X_{1}=Y_{1}\}
under
H
(as
\{X_{2}=Y_{2}\in D\}\subset H
). But now,

1-9\epsilon\leq 1-\Psi(H^{c}\,|\,Y_{1}=\xi)\leq\frac{\Psi(H\,|\,Y_{1}=\xi)}{\Psi(H\,|\,X_{1}=\xi)}\leq\frac{1}{1-\Psi(H^{c}\,|\,X_{1}=\xi)}\leq\frac{1}{1-9\epsilon}.

∎

Appendix C
A relative density bound for monotonic measures

We prove here a small Lemma that morally says “if one controls relative densities with maximal boundary conditions, then one control relative densities with any boundary conditions”. We stat it in a somewhat general form so that it can be re-used in the future.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
\Lambda
be a finite set, and
q\geq 1
be an integer. Let
\mu,\nu
be measures on
\Omega_{\Lambda}=(\{0,1\}^{q})^{\Lambda}
with mass functions (
\omega=(\omega_{i,k})_{i\in\Lambda,k=1,\dots,q}
)

\mu(\omega)=w_{\mu}(\omega)f_{\Lambda}(\omega),\quad\nu(\omega)=w_{\nu}(\omega)f_{\Lambda}(\omega),

where

f_{\Lambda}(\omega)=\prod_{i\in\Lambda}f_{i}\big((\omega_{i,k})_{k=1}^{q}\big),\quad f_{i}\big((\omega_{i,k})_{k=1}^{q}\big)=\prod_{k=1}^{q-1}\mathds{1}_{\omega_{i,k}\leq\omega_{i,k+1}},

and
w_{\mu},w_{\nu}
are positive lattice FKG functions. Let
\epsilon\in[0,1)
,
\Delta\subset W\subset\Lambda
. Suppose that
1
1

1

We denote
0,1\in\{0,1\}^{q}
for the constant
0,1
sequence.
: for
X\sim\mu
,
Y\sim\nu
,

(1)

P(X_{W}\in\cdot\,|\,X_{\Lambda\setminus W}=1)=P(Y_{W}\in\cdot\,|\,Y_{\Lambda\setminus W}=1)
,

(2)

P(X_{W}\in\cdot\,|\,X_{\Lambda\setminus W}=0)=P(Y_{W}\in\cdot\,|\,Y_{\Lambda\setminus W}=0)
,

(3)

for any
A\subset\Omega_{\Delta}
,

1-\epsilon\leq\frac{P(X_{\Delta}\in A\,|\,X_{\Lambda\setminus W}=1)}{P(X_{\Delta}\in A\,|\,X_{\Lambda\setminus W}=0)}\leq 1+\epsilon
,

where
\frac{0}{0}=1
.
Then, for any
A\subset(\{0,1\}^{q})^{\Delta}
,

\frac{1-\epsilon}{(1+\epsilon)^{2}}\leq\frac{P(X_{\Delta}\in A)}{P(Y_{\Delta}\in A)}\leq\frac{(1+\epsilon)^{2}}{1-\epsilon},

where again
\frac{0}{0}=1
.

Proof.

We refer to
[
GHM01
, Section 4]
for more details and terminology about monotonic/lattice FKG measures. Let
X\sim\mu
,
Y\sim\nu
. Note that
f
is lattice FKG, so
\mu,\nu
are lattice FKG. Note moreover that
\mu,\nu
are supported on an irreducible sub-lattice of
(\{0,1\}^{q})^{V}
, so for any
\Lambda\subset V
, and almost every realisations
\eta\leq\eta^{\prime}
of
X_{V\setminus\Lambda}
,

P(X_{\Lambda}\in\cdot\,|\,X_{V\setminus\Lambda}=\eta)\preccurlyeq P(X_{\Lambda}\in\cdot\,|\,X_{V\setminus\Lambda}=\eta^{\prime}),

and the same for
Y
. It is sufficient to show the claim for
A
being a singleton with positive
\mu
-probability (and therefore positive
\nu
-probability). Let
\Delta\supset F_{1}\supset\dots\supset F_{q}
,
E_{i}=\Delta\setminus F_{i}
. Then, letting

\displaystyle A_{X}^{1}=\cap_{i=1}^{q}\{X_{E_{i}}(i)=1\},\quad A_{X}^{0}=\cap_{i=1}^{q}\{X_{F_{i}}(i)=0\},

\displaystyle A_{Y}^{1}=\cap_{i=1}^{q}\{Y_{E_{i}}(i)=1\},\quad A_{Y}^{0}=\cap_{i=1}^{q}\{Y_{F_{i}}(i)=0\},

one has

\displaystyle\frac{P(\cap_{i=1}^{q}\{X_{F_{i}}(i)=0,X_{E_{i}}(i)=1\})}{P(\cap_{i=1}^{q}\{Y_{F_{i}}(i)=0,Y_{E_{i}}(i)=1\})}=\frac{P(A_{X}^{1})P(A_{X}^{0}\,|\,A_{X}^{1})}{P(A_{Y}^{1})P(A_{Y}^{0}\,|\,A_{Y}^{1})}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=1)P(A_{X}^{0}\,|\,A_{X}^{1},\ X_{V\setminus W}=0)}{P(A_{Y}^{1}\,|\,Y_{V\setminus W}=0)P(A_{Y}^{0}\,|\,A_{Y}^{1},\ Y_{V\setminus W}=1)}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=1)P(A_{X}^{0},\ A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=0)P(A_{Y}^{1}\,|\,Y_{V\setminus W}=1)}{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=0)P(A_{Y}^{1}\,|\,Y_{V\setminus W}=0)P(A_{Y}^{0},\ A_{Y}^{1}\,|\,Y_{V\setminus W}=1)}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=1)}{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=0)}\frac{P(A_{X}^{0},\ A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=0)}{P(A_{X}^{0},\ A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=1)}\frac{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=1)}{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=0)}\leq\frac{(1+\epsilon)^{2}}{1-\epsilon}

where we used the one-monotonicity of
\mu,\nu
in the second line, definition of conditional probability in the third, the first two hypotheses of the Lemma in the fourth line, and the last hypotheses in the last inequality. The lower bound follows the same path:

\displaystyle\frac{P(\cap_{i=1}^{q}\{X_{F_{i}}(i)=0,X_{E_{i}}(i)=1\})}{P(\cap_{i=1}^{q}\{Y_{F_{i}}(i)=0,Y_{E_{i}}(i)=1\})}\geq\frac{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=0)P(A_{X}^{0}\,|\,A_{X}^{1},\ X_{V\setminus W}=1)}{P(A_{Y}^{1}\,|\,Y_{V\setminus W}=1)P(A_{Y}^{0}\,|\,A_{Y}^{1},\ Y_{V\setminus W}=0)}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=0)}{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=1)}\frac{P(A_{X}^{0},\ A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=1)}{P(A_{X}^{0},\ A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=0)}\frac{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=0)}{P(A_{X}^{1}\,|\,X_{V\setminus W}=1)}\geq\frac{1-\epsilon}{(1+\epsilon)^{2}}.

∎
